  \frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{lv}} - \gamma}{2k}, \\
  \frac{1/2 + \epsilon_{\mathrm{lv}} - \gamma - ke}{4k}, \frac{4/3 + \epsilon_{\mathrm{lv}} - \gamma - 2ke}{4k}\Bigr),
\end{multline*}
\begin{multline*}
  \Gamma_k(\theta_-, \theta_+) = \epsilon_{\mathrm{lv}} + \max\Bigl(2k\theta_+\Bigl(\frac15 +
  \epsilon_{\mathrm{lv}}\Bigr),
  \frac12 - k\theta_-\Bigl(\frac15 - 4\epsilon_{\mathrm{lv}}\Bigr), \\
  \frac43 - k\theta_-\Bigl(\frac65 - 4\epsilon_{\mathrm{lv}}\Bigr)\Bigr).
\end{multline*}
For $k \ge 1$ and $0 \le \theta_- \le s \le \theta_+$ with $k\theta_- \ge 2/3 + \epsilon_{\mathrm{lv}}$, if $\gamma >
\Gamma_k(\theta_-, \theta_+)$, then every size $t$ with $\gamma \le \mathfrak v_k(s, t - \tau)$ has
\[
  s(t - 1) - s\tau \le \operatorname{LV}_k(\gamma; \theta_-) \tag{LV}
\]
(\S S7). The first entry of $\operatorname{LV}_k$ covers the sizes with $t - \tau < 7/10 + \epsilon_{\mathrm{lv}}$,
where $\mathfrak v_k = 1$; the other three come from the three terms of $\mathfrak r_k$, solved for $s(t - 1 - \tau)$.
The threshold $\Gamma_k(\theta_-, \theta_+)$ is the largest value of $\mathfrak r_k(s, 4/5 - \epsilon_{\mathrm{lv}}) +
\epsilon_{\mathrm{lv}}$ for $s \in [\theta_-, \theta_+]$, so that for $\gamma$ above it every such size has $t - \tau <
4/5 - \epsilon_{\mathrm{lv}}$.
% Library name: SieveResTest.lv_bound

\emph{The large values' condition.} The test applies (LV) to each member of the rest through a \emph{large values'
condition}, which depends on an exponent $e$ and an interval $[\theta_-, \theta_+]$ of thresholds. Let $k_0 = \lceil
1/e \rceil$ ($k_0 = 0$ if $e = 0$), and take the two powers $k = k_0 - 1$ and $k = k_0 - 2$ (for $e > 0$, the two
largest $k$ with $ke < 1$). For such a power and an interval $[\alpha, \alpha'] \subseteq [0, 1]$ with ends in
$2^{-64}\mathbb Z$, let $U$ be an upward rounding of $\operatorname{LV}_k(\cdot\,; e)$ in the fixed point if
\[
  k \ge 1, \qquad k\theta_- \ge 2/3 + \epsilon_{\mathrm{lv}}, \qquad \alpha > \text{an upward rounding of }
  \Gamma_k(\theta_-, \theta_+),
\]
and $U = 0$ otherwise. The condition $\mathfrak L(s, t, \gamma)$ asks that, for both powers and every such interval
that contains $\gamma$, $s(t - 1) - s\tau$ be at most the chord of $U$ between $\alpha$ and $\alpha'$, taken at
$\gamma$. Of the roundings the proofs below use only that they are at least $\operatorname{LV}_k(\cdot\,; e)$ and
$\Gamma_k(\theta_-, \theta_+)$.
% Library names: SieveResTest.lvVal, SieveResTest.lvG, SieveResTest.lvVal_ge, SieveResTest.lvGv_ge

Two cases are used, for a cell $[a, b]$ with ends in $2^{-64}\mathbb Z$ and $b > 0$, and for $v \in 2^{-64}\mathbb Z$:
\begin{itemize}
\item the condition \emph{at the upper end}, $\mathfrak L^+_{a,b}$, has $e = b$ and $[\theta_-, \theta_+] = [a, b]$,
  the thresholds of the cell;
\item the condition \emph{at the lower end}, $\mathfrak L^-_{a,v}$, has $e = a$ and $[\theta_-, \theta_+] = [a, a +
  v]$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:two-corners} $v$ is at least the width of every cell, so that $[a, a + v]$ contains the cell.
\end{itemize}

\emph{The bound from $m_+$ on.} Let $m_+$ be real, $\sigma_1, \dots, \sigma_K \ge 0$ and $\mathfrak L_1, \dots,
\mathfrak L_K$ large values' conditions. The \emph{bound holds from $m_+$ on, at the exponents $\sigma_j$, with the
conditions $\mathfrak L_j$}, if
\[
  \sum_{j=1}^K s_j(t_j - 1) + \frac\gamma2 + \operatorname{cl}(\gamma, m) \le -\frac1{25000}
\]
for every $m \ge m_+$ and all reals $s_j$, $\beta$, $\gamma$ and $t_j$ with
\begin{gather*}
  s_j \ge \sigma_j, \qquad \sum_{j=1}^K s_j \le L, \qquad 1/2 \le \beta \le 11/12, \qquad \gamma \le g_{\mathrm
  H}(\beta), \\
  t_j \le \beta + 4 \cdot 10^{-6}, \qquad \gamma \le \mathfrak m_k(s_j, t_j - \tau), \qquad \mathfrak
  L_j(s_j, t_j, \gamma),
\end{gather*}
for every $1 \le j \le K$ and every $k \ge 1$.
% Library name: SieveResTest.GoalAtV
These are the conditions on an admissible tuple in Definition~\ref{def:res}, except that the large values enter
through the conditions $\mathfrak L_j$, not through $\mathfrak v_k$. From $m = 1$ on the clamp is $0$, and the bound
is $\sum_j s_j(t_j - 1) + \gamma/2 \le
-1/25000$.

\begin{lemma}[the two corners]\label{lem:two-corners}
% Lean: SieveResTest.two_corner, SieveResTest.clamp_step, SieveResTest.carry_term, SieveResTest.carry_le, SieveResTest.mom_carry, SieveResTest.max4_shift, SieveResTest.lvU_carry, SieveResTest.lvok_carry, SieveResTest.lvok_mkTv, SieveResTest.binOK_of
Let $L$ be real, $w \ge 0$ and $K \ge 1$. For $1 \le j \le K$ let $a_j \le b_j$ be in $2^{-64}\mathbb Z$, $a_j \ge 0$
and
$b_j > 0$, and let $v$ and $m_+ \ge 0$ be in $2^{-64}\mathbb Z$ with $b_j \le a_j + v$ for every $j$. Write $\Xi =
\sum_j (b_j - a_j)$ for the box's width. Suppose that
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(U)] the bound holds from $m_+$ on, at the exponents $b_j$, with the conditions $\mathfrak L^+_{a_j, b_j}$;
\item[(Lo)] the bound holds from $1$ on, at the exponents $a_j$, with the conditions $\mathfrak L^-_{a_j, v}$;
\item[(Rm)] $m_+ + \Xi/11 + \sum_j b_j + w \le L$.
\end{enumerate}
Then $\operatorname{Res}_L(m; s_1, \dots, s_K)$ holds for all reals $s_j \in [a_j, b_j]$ and $m$ with $\sum_j s_j + m
\ge
L - w$. In particular $\operatorname{Res}_L$ holds on the box $\prod_j [a_j, b_j]$ with the variable tied to the rest,
with the margin $w$.
\end{lemma}

The upper corner is $s_j = b_j$, with the variable $m_+$, shorter by at least $\Xi/11 + w$ than the upper ends leave;
the
lower corner has the conditions at the lower ends, $\mathfrak L^-_{a_j, v}$, and no clamp. The proof carries an
admissible tuple at a point $\mathbf s$
of the box to the upper corner, where its exponent $Y$ is lower by half the distance $\Theta = \sum_j (b_j - s_j)$, while
the variable at
$\mathbf s$ is longer than $m_+$ by at least $(12/11)\Theta$; the clamp falls with it.

\begin{proof}
Fix $\mathbf s$ and $m$ as in the statement and an admissible tuple $(\beta, \gamma, \mathbf t)$ at $\mathbf s$
(Definition~\ref{def:res}). By Lemma~\ref{lem:clamp} it suffices to prove $Y + \gamma/2 + \operatorname{cl}(\gamma, m)
\le
-1/25000$, $Y = \sum_j s_j(t_j - 1)$. By (Rm), $\sum_j s_j \le \sum_j b_j \le L$, and $0 \le \Theta \le \Xi$.

\emph{The lower corner.} The tuple satisfies the hypotheses of (Lo) at $\mathbf s$ and $m = 1$: $s_j \ge a_j$, $\sum_j
s_j \le L$, the moments' conditions are those of the tuple, and the tuple has $\mathfrak L^-_{a_j, v}(s_j, t_j,
\gamma)$. Where $U = 0$ the condition reads $s_j(t_j - 1) - s_j\tau \le 0$, true as $t_j \le \beta + 4 \cdot 10^{-6}
< 1$. Where $U \ne 0$, (LV) with $\theta_- = a_j \le s_j \le b_j \le \theta_+ = a_j + v$, $\gamma \ge \alpha >
\Gamma_k(a_j, a_j + v)$ and the tuple's $\gamma \le \mathfrak v_k(s_j, t_j - \tau)$ gives $s_j(t_j - 1) - s_j\tau \le
\operatorname{LV}_k(\gamma; a_j)$, which is below the chord of $U$ as in the last item of the carry below. As
$\operatorname{cl} \ge 0$,
\[
  Y + \gamma/2 \le -1/25000. \tag{a}
\]

\emph{The carry.} Put $t'_j = 1/2 + (s_j/b_j)(t_j - 1/2)$, so $b_j(t'_j - 1/2) = s_j(t_j - 1/2)$: the excess over the
square root is kept. The tuple $(\beta, \gamma, \mathbf t')$ of reals is checked against the hypotheses of (U) at the
exponents $b_j$:
\begin{itemize}
\item $b_j(t'_j - 1) = s_j(t_j - 1) - (b_j - s_j)/2$, so $\sum_j b_j(t'_j - 1) = Y - \Theta/2$.
\item $t'_j \le \beta + 4 \cdot 10^{-6}$: since $0 \le s_j/b_j \le 1$, $t'_j \le t_j$ if $t_j \ge 1/2$, and $t'_j \le
1/2
  \le \beta$ otherwise.
\item The moments: $kb_j(1 - 2t'_j) = ks_j(1 - 2t_j)$, so
  $\mathfrak m_k(b_j, t'_j - \tau) = \max(1, kb_j) + ks_j(1 - 2t_j) + 2\tau kb_j \ge \mathfrak m_k(s_j, t_j - \tau) \ge
  \gamma$ for every $k \ge 1$, as $s_j \le b_j$.
\item The large values: $\mathfrak L^+_{a_j, b_j}(b_j, t'_j, \gamma)$. Take a power $k$ and an interval $[\alpha,
  \alpha'] \ni \gamma$. If its function is $0$, the claim is $b_j(t'_j - 1) - b_j\tau \le 0$, true as $t'_j < 1$ by the
  second item and $\beta \le 11/12$.
  Otherwise $k \ge 1$, $ka_j \ge 2/3 + \epsilon_{\mathrm{lv}}$ and $\gamma \ge \alpha > \Gamma_k(a_j, b_j)$. Then $ks_j
  \ge ka_j$, and $\Gamma_k(s_j, b_j) \le \Gamma_k(a_j, b_j)$, since its second and third entries decrease in the lower
  end. The bound (LV) with $\theta_- = s_j$, $\theta_+ = b_j$ and the tuple's $\gamma \le \mathfrak v_k(s_j, t_j -
  \tau)$ gives $s_j(t_j - 1) - s_j\tau \le \operatorname{LV}_k(\gamma; s_j)$. Entry by entry,
  $\operatorname{LV}_k(\gamma;
  s_j) - (b_j - s_j)/2 \le \operatorname{LV}_k(\gamma; b_j)$: the first entry falls by $(b_j - s_j)(3/10 -
  \epsilon_{\mathrm{lv}}) \le (b_j - s_j)/2$, the second does not change, the third falls by $(b_j - s_j)/4$ and the
  fourth by $(b_j - s_j)/2$. So
  \[
    b_j(t'_j - 1) - b_j\tau \le s_j(t_j - 1) - s_j\tau - \frac{b_j - s_j}2 \le \operatorname{LV}_k(\gamma; b_j).
  \]
  As a maximum of affine functions of $\gamma$, $\operatorname{LV}_k(\cdot\,; b_j)$ is convex, so it is below its chord
  between $\alpha$ and $\alpha'$, and the rounded values at the ends are above it.
\end{itemize}
So $(\beta, \gamma, \mathbf t')$ satisfies the hypotheses of (U) at the exponents $b_j$, with $\sum_j b_j \le L$, and
$m = m_+$:
\[
  Y - \Theta/2 + \gamma/2 + \operatorname{cl}(\gamma, m_+) \le -1/25000. \tag{b}
\]

\emph{The variable.} Since $\sum_j s_j = \sum_j b_j - \Theta$, (Rm) and $\Theta \le \Xi$ give
\[
  m \ge L - w - \sum_j b_j + \Theta \ge m_+ + \frac\Xi{11} + \Theta \ge m_+ + \frac{12}{11}\Theta.
\]

\emph{The clamp falls.} Write $\operatorname{cl}_1(m) = \max(1 - m, 0)/2$ and $\operatorname{cl}_2(m) = \max(0, \gamma/2
+ y_0(m))$ for the clamp's two entries. As $m - m_+ \ge
\Theta \ge 0$, $\operatorname{cl}_1(m) \le \max(\operatorname{cl}_1(m_+) - \Theta/2, 0)$. As $(12/11)(11/12 + \delta')
\ge 1$,
\begin{multline*}
  y_0(m) = y_0(m_+) - (m - m_+)\Bigl(\frac{11}{12} + \delta'\Bigr)\Big/2 \\
  \le y_0(m_+) - \frac{12}{11}\Bigl(\frac{11}{12} + \delta'\Bigr)\frac\Theta2 \le y_0(m_+) - \frac\Theta2,
\end{multline*}
so $\operatorname{cl}_2(m) \le \max(\operatorname{cl}_2(m_+) - \Theta/2, 0)$. The clamp at $m_+$ is
$\operatorname{cl}_i(m_+)$ for some $i$, and then
$\operatorname{cl}(\gamma, m) \le \operatorname{cl}_i(m) \le \max(\operatorname{cl}(\gamma, m_+) - \Theta/2, 0)$. If this
maximum is $\operatorname{cl}(\gamma, m_+) - \Theta/2$, (b) gives $Y + \gamma/2 + \operatorname{cl}(\gamma, m) \le
-1/25000$; if it is $0$, (a) does.
\end{proof}

The upper bound $\sum_j s_j + m \le L + 10^{-5}$ is not used: a longer variable only lowers the clamp. The factor
$12/11$ is a simple value at which the clamp's flatter entry, of slope $(11/12 + \delta')/2$ in $m$, falls by at least
$\Theta/2$; the
shortening by $\Xi/11 \ge \Theta/11$ supplies the excess over $\Theta$ at every point of the box. The upper corner
takes the thresholds $[a_j, b_j]$ of the whole cell because (LV) is applied at the tuple's own exponent $s_j$, which
may be anywhere in the cell, and $\Gamma_k(s_j, b_j) \le \Gamma_k(a_j, b_j)$.

The evaluation of a term needs the condition on a quadrant (Proposition~\ref{prop:tuples-rest}). The next lemma cuts
the box into sub-cells thin enough that on each the quadrant's condition follows from the tied one, and gives the tuple
estimate on the box. Here $k$ is again the length of a tuple, as in Section~\ref{sec:window-terms}, not a power: a
tuple has $k$ primes, $K$ of which make up the rest.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:tied-to-tuples}
% Lean: SieveTypeIIHM.fullEst_of_tight, SieveTypeIIHM.resBox_of_tight, SieveTypeIIHM.full_res_exp, SieveCurveHx.ghx_le_one, SieveCurveHx.ghx_half, SieveCurveHx.counts_hx
Under Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}, let $0 < L \le 5/2$, $e_0 > 0$, $K \ge 1$, and $0 \le a_j \le b_j$
for $1 \le j \le K$ with $\sum_j b_j + 10^{-5} \le L$. If $\operatorname{Res}_L$ holds on the box $\prod_j [a_j - 2
\cdot 10^{-5}, b_j + 2 \cdot 10^{-5}]$ with the variable tied to the rest, with the margin $2 \cdot 10^{-5}$, then the
tuple estimate (Definition~\ref{def:tuple-estimate}) holds at $(L, e_0)$ for the region $\mathcal G_q$ of the
$K$-tuples $\mathbf y$ with $q^{a_j} \le y_j \le q^{b_j}$, $1 \le j \le K$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $k$, $J_0$ and $\Lambda_1 \ge 0$ be given. Put $\eta = 1/(4 \cdot 10^5 K)$, so $K\eta = 2.5 \cdot 10^{-6}$. For
$\mathbf n$ in the finite set $\mathcal J = \prod_j \{0, 1, \dots, \lfloor (b_j - a_j)/\eta \rfloor\}$ let
$A_j(\mathbf n) = a_j + n_j\eta \in [a_j, b_j]$, the sub-cell $[A_j, A_j + \eta)$, and $\rho_2(\mathbf n) = \sum_j
(A_j(\mathbf n) + \eta)$.

\emph{A sub-cell.} $\operatorname{Res}_L$ holds on the quadrant $(L - \rho_2(\mathbf n) - \eta; A_1 - \eta, \dots, A_K
- \eta)$ of Definition~\ref{def:res-quadrant}. Let $m \ge L - \rho_2(\mathbf n) - \eta$ and $s_j \ge A_j - \eta$ with
$\sum_j s_j + m \le L + 10^{-5}$. Then $s_j \ge a_j - 2 \cdot 10^{-5}$; and
\[
  s_j - (A_j - \eta) \le \sum_i (s_i - A_i + \eta) \le L + 10^{-5} - m - \sum_i A_i + K\eta \le 10^{-5} + 2K\eta + \eta,
\]
so $s_j \le b_j + 10^{-5} + 2K\eta \le b_j + 2 \cdot 10^{-5}$. Also $\sum_j s_j + m \ge \sum_j (A_j - \eta) + L -
\rho_2(\mathbf n) - \eta = L - 2K\eta - \eta \ge L - 2 \cdot 10^{-5}$. So $(m; \mathbf s)$ is in the widened box with
the variable tied, and $\operatorname{Res}_L(m; \mathbf s)$ holds.

\emph{The estimate of a sub-cell.} For each $\mathbf n$, Proposition~\ref{prop:tuples-rest} with $k$, $J_0 + 2K$
comparisons, $\Lambda_1 + L$, $\rho_2 = \rho_2(\mathbf n) \ge 0$, $\varepsilon = \eta$, $\sigma_j = A_j(\mathbf n)$ and
$\rho_1 = 0$ applies, since $L - \rho_2(\mathbf n) - \eta \ge L - \sum_j b_j - K\eta - \eta \ge 10^{-5} - 5 \cdot
10^{-6} > 0$; it gives $\delta_{\mathbf n} > 0$ and $q_{\mathbf n}$. Let $\delta_0 = \min_{\mathbf n} \delta_{\mathbf
n}$, and take $q_0 \ge \max_{\mathbf n} q_{\mathbf n}$ such that $|\mathcal J| \le q^{\delta_0/2}$ for $q \ge q_0$.

\emph{The cut.} Let $q \ge q_0$, $a$, the rest's indices $i_1, \dots, i_K$ and comparisons be as in
Definition~\ref{def:tuple-estimate}. Each tuple $\mathbf z$ that satisfies them has $q^{a_j} \le z_{i_j} \le q^{b_j}$,
so $n_j(\mathbf z) = \lfloor (\log_q z_{i_j} - a_j)/\eta \rfloor$ defines $\mathbf n(\mathbf z) \in \mathcal J$, and
$\mathbf n(\mathbf z) = \mathbf n$ if and only if the $2K$ comparisons $z_{i_j} \ge q^{A_j(\mathbf n)}$ and $z_{i_j} <
q^{A_j(\mathbf n) + \eta}$ hold. Their constant terms are $-A_j \log q \le 0$ and $(A_j + \eta)\log q \le L\log q$,
since $A_j + \eta \le b_j + 10^{-5} \le L$. The sum over the tuples is the sum over $\mathbf n \in \mathcal J$ of the
sums over the families with these $2K$ comparisons added. In the family of $\mathbf n$ every tuple has its entries at
least $q^{e_0}$, $z_{i_j} \ge q^{A_j}$, $1 \le \prod_j z_{i_j} \le q^{\rho_2(\mathbf n)}$ and its product in $(N_1,
N_2]$, and $\operatorname{Res}_L$ holds on the quadrant above; so its sum is at most $q^{L - \delta_{\mathbf
n}}/\varphi(q) \le q^{L - \delta_0}/\varphi(q)$. Summing over $\mathcal J$ gives at most $q^{L -
\delta_0/2}/\varphi(q)$, and $\delta = \delta_0/2$.
\end{proof}

In Lemma~\ref{lem:two-corners} the cells are the box's cells widened by $2 \cdot 10^{-5}$ and rounded outward to
$2^{-64}\mathbb Z$, so the two lemmas together turn a passing test (W$'$) into the tuple estimate on the box
(Lemma~\ref{lem:window-kernel}(b) and Lemma~\ref{lem:window-tests}).

\subsection{The tests of the window}\label{sec:window-tests}

The window evaluates the term of a box when a rest, a set of its primes (Section~\ref{sec:window-terms}), satisfies the
resonance condition (Definition~\ref{def:res}) on the exponents that the box's cells allow. This subsection writes that
condition as two tests, (W) and (W$'$), on a box and as (W$_{\mathcal M}$) and (W$'_{\mathcal M}$) on a chain over a
frozen part (Definition~\ref{def:window-tests}). Lemma~\ref{lem:window-tests} proves that a box that passes them has
its term evaluated, and that a chain that passes them has its term over the frozen part $o(X)$;
Lemma~\ref{lem:window-kernel} gives the two integer tests that Lean's kernel decides and that imply them. The condition
is stated in the units of Section~\ref{sec:decomp}: a cell $(l, h)$ is in units of $1/D$ of exponents of $x$, and $Le/D$
is the exponent of $q$ of the unit $e$. Throughout, $\mathfrak b$ is a box of $k \ge 1$ primes with cells $(l_i, h_i)$,
the curve is $g_{\mathrm H}$, and for a nonempty set $\mathcal S_r$ of indices, $l(\mathcal S_r)$ and $h(\mathcal S_r)$
are as in Section~\ref{sec:decomp}.

\emph{The cofactor.} The cofactor $n/(p_1 \cdots p_k)$ of a counted $n$ is one prime when the box's cells leave room
for one prime and no more:
\[
  (\mathrm{Cf}) \qquad h(\mathfrak b) \le D - 2, \qquad l(\mathfrak b) + 2l_k \ge D + 1,
\]
the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:term-rest-cof}(c). For large $q$ that lemma gives $p_1 \cdots p_k \le N_1$ and $N_2 <
p_1 \cdots p_k\, p_k^2$, and puts the cofactor of a counted $n$, a prime $y \ge p_k$, possibly equal to $p_k$, in the
cell $c_* = (l_*, h_*)$, here with both ends included ($x^{l_*/D} \le y \le x^{h_*/D}$), with $l_* = \max(D -
h(\mathfrak b) - 1, l_k)$ and $h_* = D - l(\mathfrak b) + 1$.
Over a frozen part $\mathcal M(e_-, e_+)$ (Section~\ref{sec:reversal}), $R$ is the product of the frozen primes $a_2,
\dots, a_k$ and of the frozen cofactor, an integer with no prime factor below $a_k$, and $\Delta_{\mathcal M}(\mathfrak
c)$ is the term over $\mathcal M$ of a chain $\mathfrak c$. For a chain $\mathfrak c$ of $j \ge 1$ primes with cells
$(l'_i, h'_i)$, the chain's cofactor $k' = n/(R p'_1 \cdots p'_j)$ is one prime when
\[
  (\mathrm{Cf}_{\mathcal M}) \qquad e_+ + h'(\mathfrak c) \le D - 2, \qquad e_- + l'(\mathfrak c) + 2l'_j \ge D + 1,
\]
by the argument of that lemma with $R \in (x^{e_-/D}, x^{e_+/D}]$ (\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak frozCof\_facts}), and
its cell is $c_* = (l_*, h_*)$ with $l_* = \max(D - e_+ - h'(\mathfrak c) - 1, l'_j)$ and $h_* = \max(D - e_- -
l'(\mathfrak c), 0) + 1$.
% Lean: SieveAd.cof_facts, SieveAd.frozCof_facts, SieveAd.big_facts

\begin{definition}\label{def:window-tests}
% Lean: SieveTypeIIHM.ResBox, SieveTypeIIHM.ResBoxT; the conditions as the kernel reads them: SieveAd.chkIRCH, SieveAd.chkIRY, SieveAd.chkIRFH, SieveAd.chkIRFY, SieveAd.rpass, SieveAd.rpassY, SieveAd.headLo
Let $\mathfrak b$ be a box of $k \le 13$ primes with $1 \le l_i \le h_i \le D$ for every $i$. A \emph{rest} is a
nonempty set $\mathcal S_r$ of its indices, or, when $(\mathrm{Cf})$ holds, such a set together with the cofactor, whose
cell is then $c_*$. Let $c_1, \dots, c_K$ be the lower ends of the rest's cells and $d_1, \dots, d_K$ their upper ends,
$K = |\mathcal S_r|$ or $|\mathcal S_r| + 1$, and $H = d_1 + \dots + d_K$; so $H = h(\mathcal S_r)$, or $H =
h(\mathcal S_r) + h_*$ with the cofactor. The box passes the \emph{tests of the window} for the rest as follows:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(W)] if $H + 1 < D$ and $\operatorname{Res}_L$ holds on the quadrant $(m_0; \sigma_1, \dots, \sigma_K)$
  (Definition~\ref{def:res-quadrant}), with the least exponent $m_0 = L(D - H - 1)/D$ of the variable and the lower
  ends $\sigma_j = L(c_j - 1)/D$ of the rest;
\item[(W$'$)] if $c_j \le d_j$ for every $j$, $\sum_j Ld_j/D + 10^{-5} \le L$, and $\operatorname{Res}_L$ holds on the
  box $\prod_j [Lc_j/D - 2 \cdot 10^{-5}, Ld_j/D + 2 \cdot 10^{-5}]$ with the variable tied to the rest, with the margin
  $w = 2 \cdot 10^{-5}$ (Definition~\ref{def:res-tied}): the box of the rest's cells, widened by $2 \cdot 10^{-5}$.
\end{enumerate}
Each test is \emph{with the cofactor} when the rest contains it.

Let $\mathcal M = \mathcal M(e_-, e_+)$ be a frozen
part of a box of $k \ge 2$ primes, and $\mathfrak c$ a chain of $j \ge 1$ primes, with $(k - 1) + j \le 13$ and $1 \le
l \le h \le D$ for all the cells of $\mathfrak c$ and of $\mathfrak b'$. A rest is a nonempty set of the variables
$a_2, \dots, a_k$, $p'_1, \dots, p'_j$, each in its cell, or, when $(\mathrm{Cf}_{\mathcal M})$ holds, such a set
together
with the chain's cofactor $k'$, in its cell $c_*$. The chain passes (W$_{\mathcal M}$) or (W$'_{\mathcal M}$) over
$\mathcal M$ if the condition of (W) or of (W$'$) holds on the cells of the rest.
\end{definition}

In (W) the \emph{variable} is all of $n$ outside the rest: the box's other primes and, when the rest does not contain
the cofactor, the
cofactor, whatever its number of primes. Its exponent is bounded only from below, through the rest's upper ends: $n >
N_1 \ge x^{(D - 1)/D}$ and the rest is at most $x^{H/D}$. The cells of the variable's primes are not read, and of the
rest only the lower ends and the sum of the upper ends, each with a margin of one unit. In (W$'$) the rest's members
are read in their cells, both ends, and the variable is tied to them: it is what they leave of $x$, within $2 \cdot
10^{-5}$ below and $10^{-5}$ above in exponents of $q$. With the cofactor, (W) asks $h(\mathcal S_r) + 3 \le
l(\mathfrak b)$, so its rest leaves out at least one prime of the box.

In (W$_{\mathcal M}$) and (W$'_{\mathcal M}$) the frozen cofactor is always in the variable, with the chain's cofactor
when it is not in the rest. The frozen primes $a_2, \dots, a_k$ enter the tests through their cells when they are in
the rest. The range $(x^{e_-/D}, x^{e_+/D}]$ of $R$ enters only through $(\mathrm{Cf}_{\mathcal M})$ and $c_*$.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:window-tests}
% Lean: (a) SieveAd.evI_resCEH, SieveAd.evI_resEH, SieveAd.evI_resYE, SieveAd.cof_facts, SieveTypeIIHM.chain_res_exp, SieveTypeIIHM.chain_res_cof, SieveTypeIIHM.chain_res_gen, SieveTypeIIHM.chain_res_cofG, SieveTypeIIHM.fullEst_of_tight; (b) SieveAd.evIF_froz_res, SieveAd.evIF_froz_resC, SieveAd.evIF_froz_gen, SieveAd.evIF_froz_genC, SieveAd.evIF_frozP_res, SieveAd.evIF_frozP_resC, SieveAd.frozCof_facts
Assume Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}, and let $2 < L \le 5/2$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] The term of a box of $k \ge 1$ primes that passes (W) or (W$'$), with or without the cofactor, is
  evaluated; and it is so uniformly when the family is cut by at most $40 - 3k$ further comparisons, as in
  Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(c): $q_0$ does not depend on the comparisons.
\item[(b)] Let $\mathcal M = \mathcal M(e_-, e_+)$ be a frozen part of a box of $k \ge 2$ primes, with $0 \le e_-, e_+
  \le D$; in particular the wide and the narrow parts when $1 \le l_1 \le h_1 \le D$. If a chain $\mathfrak c$ of $j \ge
  1$ primes passes
  (W$_{\mathcal M}$) or (W$'_{\mathcal M}$), with or without the chain's cofactor, then $\Delta_{\mathcal M}(\mathfrak
  c) = o(X)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

The proof of (a) is that of Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(a), with Lemma~\ref{lem:term-rest} or~\ref{lem:term-rest-cof} in
place of Lemma~\ref{lem:term-two}; the proof of (b) is that of Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tests}, with
Proposition~\ref{prop:tuples-rest} in place of Lemma~\ref{lem:chains-two}. The formalisation has no separate theorem
for (a): it proves the evaluation with the tests that Lean's kernel decides (below) as hypotheses, through (a)
(Lemma~\ref{lem:window-kernel}(c)).

\begin{proof}
(a) The family $\mathcal F(\mathfrak b)$ is the set of the $k$-tuples of primes up to $N_2$ that satisfy $3k - 1$
comparisons, $2k$ for the cells and $k - 1$ for the order, with constant terms at most $L \log q$, and the further ones
bring the number to at most $40$. Take $e_0 = L/D$, since every prime is at least $x^{1/D}$, $J_0 = 40$ and $\Lambda_1 =
L + 1$. Every tuple of the family is strictly decreasing and has $x^{l_i/D} \le p_i < x^{h_i/D}$.

(W) without the cofactor. Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:term-rest}(b) with the rest $\mathcal S_r$, $\rho_2 = Lh(\mathcal
S_r)/D$, $\varepsilon = L/D$, the lower bounds $\sigma_i = Ll_i/D$ for $i \in \mathcal S_r$ and $\rho_1 = Ll(\mathcal
S_r)/D$. Its hypotheses on the tuples hold: $p_i \ge q^{\sigma_i}$, and $q^{\rho_1} \le \prod_{\mathcal S_r} p_i \le
q^{\rho_2}$. Its condition on the quadrant, at $L - \rho_2 - \varepsilon = L(D - H - 1)/D$ and the lower ends
$\sigma_i - \varepsilon = L(l_i - 1)/D$, is (W), and $L - \rho_2 - \varepsilon > 0$ is $H + 1 < D$. Its bound
$q^{L - \delta}/\varphi(q)$ is at most $\eta X$ for large $q$.

(W) with the cofactor. By $(\mathrm{Cf})$ and Lemma~\ref{lem:term-rest-cof}(c), for large $q$ every tuple of the family
has $r =
p_1 \cdots p_k \le N_1$ and $N_2 < r p_k^2$, and every $y \ge p_k$ with $N_1 < ry \le N_2$ has $y \ge x^{l_*/D}$ and
$x^{(l(\mathcal S_r) + l_*)/D} \le y \prod_{\mathcal S_r} p_i \le x^{H/D}$, with $H = h(\mathcal S_r) + h_*$. These are
the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:term-rest-cof}(b), with the rest $\mathcal S_r$ and the cofactor's lower bound
$Ll_*/D$,
$\rho_2 = LH/D$, $\varepsilon = L/D$ and $\rho_1 = L(l(\mathcal S_r) + l_*)/D$; its condition on the quadrant is (W)
with the cofactor, and it bounds $\Delta(\mathfrak b)$ by $q^{L - \delta}/\varphi(q)$.

(W$'$). Lemma~\ref{lem:tied-to-tuples}, with the lower ends $Lc_j/D \ge 0$ and the upper ends $Ld_j/D$, turns (W$'$)
into the tuple estimate (Definition~\ref{def:tuple-estimate}) for the region $x^{c_j/D} \le z_j \le x^{d_j/D}$ of the
rest's entries, with $e_0 = L/D$. Without the cofactor, each prime of $\mathcal S_r$ lies in its cell, so in the region;
Lemma~\ref{lem:term-rest}(a) gives the bound. With the cofactor, Lemma~\ref{lem:term-rest-cof}(c) gives $r \le N_1$
and $N_2 < r p_k^2$ and puts every $y \ge p_k$ with $N_1 < ry \le N_2$ in $c_*$; with the facts on the family above and
the rest's primes in their cells, these are the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:term-rest-cof}(a).

The uniformity is that of Lemmas~\ref{lem:term-rest} and~\ref{lem:term-rest-cof}: $\delta$ and $q_0$ depend only on
$J_0$, $\Lambda_1$, the exponents and the estimate, not on the comparisons, and the family with $J$ further
comparisons has $3k - 1 + J \le 40$ of them when $J \le 40 - 3k$, a range that contains $J = 0$ as $k \le 13$.

(b) As in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tests}, write the frozen cofactor of a pair of $\mathcal M$ as a
non-increasing tuple $\mathbf y$ of $t$ primes at least $a_k$, and the chain's cofactor $k'$ as
a non-increasing tuple $\mathbf y'$ of $t'$ primes at
least $p'_j$; since every prime is at least $x^{1/D}$ and $n \le N_2 < x^{(D + 1)/D}$, $t, t' \le D$. Then
$\Delta_{\mathcal M}(\mathfrak c)$ is the sum over $t, t' \le D$ of the sums of $\vartheta(n)$ over the full tuples
$(\mathbf a, \mathbf y, \mathbf p', \mathbf y')$ of $(k - 1) + t + j + t'$ primes with product $n \in (N_1, N_2]$. Each
such family is cut out by at most $3(k - 1) + 3j + (t^2 + t) + (t'^2 + t') + 2 + 2$ comparisons, with constant terms at
most $(L + 1)\log q$; in that order, they are the cells and the order of $\mathbf a$ and of $\mathbf p'$, the order of
$\mathbf y$ and $y_i \ge a_k$, that of $\mathbf y'$ and $y'_i \ge p'_j$, the two of $R \in (x^{e_-/D}, x^{e_+/D}]$, and
the two of the range of $n$.

There is no order between $\mathbf a$, $\mathbf y$ and $\mathbf p'$; Proposition~\ref{prop:tuples-rest} asks none.
Apply it to each family, with $e_0 = L/D$, the rest at the positions of its variables among $\mathbf a$ and $\mathbf
p'$, $\rho_2 = L\overline e(\mathcal S)/D$ and $\rho_1 = L\underline e(\mathcal S)/D$ for the rest $\mathcal S$ (as in
Section~\ref{sec:reversal}, with the frozen cofactor not in $\mathcal S$), $\varepsilon = L/D$, and as lower bounds
the lower ends of the members' cells times $L/D$. Its hypotheses hold: every entry is at least $x^{1/D}$, each member
of the rest lies in its cell, and the rest's product lies in $[x^{\underline e(\mathcal S)/D}, x^{\overline e(\mathcal
S)/D}]$. Its condition is (W$_{\mathcal M}$), and it bounds each family by $q^{L - \delta}/\varphi(q)$, with $\delta$
and $q_0$ that depend on $t$, $t'$ and the fixed data only. The $(D + 1)^2$ families give $o(X)$.

With the chain's cofactor in the rest, $(\mathrm{Cf}_{\mathcal M})$ gives, for large $q$ and every tuple $(\mathbf a,
\mathbf y, \mathbf p')$, $R p'_1 \cdots p'_j \le N_1$ and $N_2 < R p'_1 \cdots p'_j\, {p'_j}^2$, so only $t' = 1$
contributes, and its one prime $y'$ lies in $[x^{l_*/D}, x^{h_*/D}]$. It joins the rest as its last member, with the
lower bound $Ll_*/D$, $\rho_2 = L(\overline e(\mathcal S) + h_*)/D$ and $\rho_1 = 0$ ($\rho_1$ does not enter the
condition), and the argument is the same. For
(W$'_{\mathcal M}$), Lemma~\ref{lem:tied-to-tuples} gives the tuple estimate for the region of the rest's cells, with or
without $c_*$, and it is applied to each family in place of Proposition~\ref{prop:tuples-rest}: every member of the
rest lies in its cell, the chain's cofactor by $(\mathrm{Cf}_{\mathcal M})$.
\end{proof}

The formalisation proves (b) for every frozen part with $e_\pm \le D$, with (W$_{\mathcal M}$) and (W$'_{\mathcal M}$)
or with the kernel's tests (below) as hypotheses; it does not prove (b) uniformly under further comparisons, and the
trees over the frozen parts do not need it. Section~\ref{sec:certificate-b} lets the nodes (E) and (E$_{\mathcal M}$) of
the trees use these tests, at the cost $0$ (Definition~\ref{def:tree-b}(i)), and carries
Proposition~\ref{prop:tree-bound} and Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tree} over to the trees that result.
% Lean: SieveAd.resTestsFYH_sound, SieveAd.rules_frozResYH, SieveAd.rules_frozResCH

\subsubsection*{What the kernel checks}

The tests (W) and (W$'$) are statements about all real exponents and all classes of characters, and the kernel cannot
decide them. It decides two integer tests that imply them, each led by a hint that it does not trust; each reads $L$,
the hint and integers in units of $1/D$, and passes or fails. The
\emph{test of the resonance condition} $\operatorname{Res}^{\mathrm k}_L(c_1, \dots, c_K; H)$ (Lean
\lean{SieveResTest.\allowbreak resXH}) reads the rest's lower ends $c_j$ and the sum $H$ of its upper ends; the
\emph{test at the two corners} $\operatorname{Res}^{\mathrm k}_L((c_1, d_1), \dots, (c_K, d_K))$ (Lean
\lean{SieveResTest.\allowbreak resYH}) reads the rest's cells. The kernel's test of an evaluation by (W) or (W$'$), with
or without the cofactor, is the conjunction of the conditions on the box of Definition~\ref{def:window-tests} other than
the resonance condition, and of the first or the second test on the rest's cells (Lean \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak
chkIRCH}, \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak chkIRY}; over a frozen part \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak chkIRFH},
\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak chkIRFY}).

\emph{The test of the resonance condition in mathematical terms.} Write $\sigma_j = L(c_j - 1)/D$ and
$m_0 = L(D - H - 1)/D$. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:clamp}, $\operatorname{Res}_L$ holds on the quadrant $(m_0; \sigma_1, \dots, \sigma_K)$ if, at every
point $(m; s_1, \dots, s_K)$ of the quadrant and for every admissible tuple $(\beta, \gamma, t_1, \dots, t_K)$ at
$(s_1, \dots, s_K)$ (Definition~\ref{def:res}),
\[
  (\star) \qquad \sum_j s_j(t_j - 1) + \frac\gamma2 + \operatorname{cl}(\gamma, m) \le -\frac1{25000},
\]
with the clamp $\operatorname{cl}$ of Section~\ref{sec:corners}, the smaller of what orthogonality and the
Hal\'asz--Montgomery inequality add for the variable. The clamp does not increase in $m$, so the test takes it at $m =
m_0$.

For $\gamma \le 0$ the half-plane gives $t_j - 1 \le \beta + 4 \cdot 10^{-6} - 1 \le
-1/12 + 4 \cdot 10^{-6}$, and one inequality, with no hint, settles every tuple with $\gamma \le 0$. Since
$g_{\mathrm H} \le 1$, $\gamma \le 1$.

The hint cuts $(0, 1]$ into at most $15$ intervals with ends on the grid $2^{-12}$. On each interval $[\alpha,
\alpha']$ it names an upper line in $\gamma$ for each member's term $s_j(t_j - 1) - s_j\tau$, with $\tau = 10^{-6}$
(Definition~\ref{def:res}), from one of
\begin{itemize}
\item the half-plane and the curve, through the bound of $\beta$ that the curve's pieces give for $\gamma \ge \alpha$;
\item the moments $\mathfrak m_{k_0 - 1}$ and $\mathfrak m_{k_0}$, $k_0 = \lceil 1/\sigma_j \rceil$ for $\sigma_j > 0$
  (the kernel takes $\sigma_j$ rounded down to the fixed point, \S S7);
\item the large values $\mathfrak v_{k_0 - 1}$ and $\mathfrak v_{k_0 - 2}$, where they apply: their bound is convex in
  $\gamma$, and its chord on $[\alpha, \alpha']$ is the line;
\end{itemize}
and one of three upper lines for $\operatorname{cl}(\gamma, m_0)$ (\S S7). The sum of the chosen lines, with
$\gamma/2$, $L\tau$ and $1/25000$, must be at most $0$ at $\gamma = \alpha$ and at $\gamma = \alpha'$; it is affine in
$\gamma$, so the two ends suffice. Everything is computed
in fixed point with the unit $2^{-64}$, every rounding in the direction that keeps the computed sum above the true one.
The test at the two corners runs the same kind of check twice, with the large values' conditions of
Section~\ref{sec:corners}: at the upper corner of the rest's cells with a shortened variable, and at the lower corner
with a variable where the clamp is $0$; Lemma~\ref{lem:two-corners} is why that suffices. The
details are in \S S7.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:window-kernel}
% Lean: SieveResTest.resXH_sound_cells, SieveResTest.resXH_hs, SieveResTest.resY_sound, SieveResTest.resY_room, SieveResTest.resYH_eq; the tests of the search: SieveAd.resTestsFYH_sound, SieveAd.evI_resCEH, SieveAd.evI_resYE; over frozen parts: SieveAd.evIF_froz_resTCH, SieveAd.evIF_froz_resTY, SieveAd.rules_frozResYH
Let $L = L_1/L_2$ with integers $L_1 \ge 0$, $L_2 \ge 1$. Whatever the hint:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] if $\operatorname{Res}^{\mathrm k}_L(c_1, \dots, c_K; H)$ passes, with integers $c_j \ge 1$ and $H \ge 0$,
  then $H + 1 < D$ and the condition on the quadrant of (W) holds;
\item[(b)] if $\operatorname{Res}^{\mathrm k}_L((c_1, d_1), \dots, (c_K, d_K))$ passes, then $c_j \le d_j$ for every
  $j$, $\sum_j Ld_j/D + 2 \cdot 10^{-5} \le L$, and the tied condition of (W$'$) holds;
\item[(c)] under Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}, for $2 < L \le 5/2$, a box of $k \le 13$ primes that
passes the kernel's test of an evaluation by (W) or
  (W$'$), with or without the cofactor, has its term evaluated, uniformly as in Lemma~\ref{lem:window-tests}(a); and a
  chain of $j \ge 1$ primes that passes the kernel's test of (W$_{\mathcal M}$) or (W$'_{\mathcal M}$) over a frozen
  part $\mathcal M(e_-, e_+)$ with $e_\pm \le D$ has $\Delta_{\mathcal M}(\mathfrak c) = o(X)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) and (b) are proved in \S S7: (a) by the two cases in $\gamma$ above, and (b) by (a)'s argument at the two corners
and Lemma~\ref{lem:two-corners}. A passing test also has $c_j \ge 1$ for the rest's lower ends, as every cell has $l
\ge 1$ and $l_* \ge 1$. So the kernel's test of an evaluation implies the conditions of
Definition~\ref{def:window-tests}; (b) gives the room $2 \cdot 10^{-5}$, more than the $10^{-5}$ that (W$'$) asks.
Then (c) is Lemma~\ref{lem:window-tests}.
\end{proof}

At $21/10$ the search of Section~\ref{sec:certificate-b} decides, besides the tests of
Definitions~\ref{def:tests} and~\ref{def:frozen-tree}, the kernel's tests of an evaluation by (W) and (W$'$), led by
the hints; under Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane}
and~\ref{inp:cgl} and for $2 < L \le 5/2$ it is sound whatever its hints (Lemma~\ref{lem:search-b}(a)), as the search
of Section~\ref{sec:certificate} is (Lemma~\ref{lem:search}).

\section{Two more rules}\label{sec:rules}

Theorem~\ref{thm:2110} is proved by the decomposition of Section~\ref{sec:decomp}, with the tests of the window of
Section~\ref{sec:window} and two more rules.

The first concerns the leaves of a comparison. The tests (J2) and (J3) of Definition~\ref{def:tests} evaluate the
leaves by Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves}, and for that they ask the leaves' condition (L). Write $d_1$ and $d_2$ for the two
products of primes of a leaf, of about $x^{a_1}$ and $x^{a_2}$. The second inequality of (L), $a_1 + a_2 < 1 -
1/(2L)$, says that the cofactor of a leaf, the integer divided by $d_1 d_2$, is longer than $x^{1/(2L)} = q^{1/2}$;
for $d_1 \ge q$ the rule of Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves} says the same. So the tests do not evaluate the leaves whose
cofactor is shorter. Such a leaf is a sum over the integers $n$ divisible by $d_1 d_2$ whose cofactor $n/(d_1 d_2)$ is
$w$-rough, and the first rule evaluates it by an estimate in two variables in which $d_1$ is the first variable, the
cofactor the second, and the primes of $d_2$ the rest. A variable shorter than $\sqrt q$, such as this cofactor, needs
a third bound for its mean square over a bin, by a fourth moment. Section~\ref{sec:leaf-estimate} proves that bound,
and the estimate on a region of the leaves. Section~\ref{sec:leaves-three} cuts the leaves of a comparison in three
ranges, two that Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves} reaches and the third range in that region, and gives the condition (L$_3$)
that the tests may ask in place of (L).

The second rule is the reversal of a term of the sign $+$. Section~\ref{sec:reversal} reverses a box of the sign $-$; a
box of the sign $+$ that no test evaluates is dropped (Lemma~\ref{lem:drop}) or decomposed.
Section~\ref{sec:reversal-plus} reverses such a box, with the roles of $\mathcal A$ and $\mathcal B$ exchanged.

\subsection{The estimate in two variables with a fourth moment}\label{sec:leaf-estimate}

This subsection proves a third bound for the mean square of a polynomial over a bin, by its fourth moment
(Lemma~\ref{lem:fourth}(b)), the estimate in two variables with it (Proposition~\ref{prop:type2-gen4}), the leaves'
region on which that estimate holds with the curve $g^*$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:type2-leaves}), and the form of it that the
sieve uses (Lemma~\ref{lem:leaf-term}).

Let $W(\chi) = \sum_{m \in S} c_m \chi(m) m^{-ih_0}$ with $S \subseteq (P, 2P]$ and $V = \sum_{m \in S} |c_m|^2$, as
in Section~\ref{sec:two-bounds}. For $P$ below $q$ orthogonality bounds the mean square over a bin by about $qV$
whatever the bin; for $P$ below $\sqrt q$ the square $W(\chi)^2$ is a polynomial of length about $P^2 \le q$, and
orthogonality applied to it gives a better bound in the bins with few characters.

\begin{lemma}[the fourth moment over the characters]\label{lem:fourth}
% Lean: SieveTypeIIHM.sum_chars_dp_four_le, SieveTypeIIHM.sum_bin_four_le, SieveTypeIIHM.card_fiber_le_pow
Let $q \ge 1$ and $P \ge 0$ be integers, $S \subseteq (P, 2P]$, $c_m \in \mathbb C$ and $h_0 \in \mathbb R$, and let
$F$ be a real number such that for every $n$ at most $F$ pairs $(j, k) \in S \times S$ have $jk = n$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] $\sum_\chi |W(\chi)|^4 \le (3P^2 + q) F V^2$, summed over all the characters mod $q$.
\item[(b)] For every set $\mathcal X$ of characters mod $q$, $\sum_{\chi \in \mathcal X} |W(\chi)|^2 \le |\mathcal
  X|^{1/2} \bigl((3P^2 + q) F\bigr)^{1/2} V$.
\item[(c)] For $0 < \varepsilon \le 1$ there is $C_\varepsilon \ge 1$, depending only on $\varepsilon$, with $d(n) \le
  C_\varepsilon n^\varepsilon$ for every $n \ge 1$, where $d(n)$ is the number of divisors of $n$; with this
  $C_\varepsilon$, $F = C_\varepsilon (4P^2)^\varepsilon$ bounds the number of pairs as above.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The proof applies the first bound of Section~\ref{sec:two-bounds} to $W(\chi)^2$. Since $0 \notin S$, $\chi(jk) =
\chi(j)\chi(k)$ and $(jk)^{-ih_0} = j^{-ih_0} k^{-ih_0}$ give $W(\chi)^2 = \sum_n c'_n \chi(n) n^{-ih_0}$, summed over
the products $n = jk$ of two elements of $S$, with $c'_n = \sum_{jk = n} c_j c_k$. These products lie in $(P^2, 4P^2]$,
an interval of length $3P^2$. The first bound of Section~\ref{sec:two-bounds} holds for a polynomial over any interval
$(A, A + M]$ with $M + q$ in place of $P + q$, with the same proof, since a residue class mod $q$ has at most $M/q + 1$
elements there. So $\sum_\chi |W(\chi)|^4 = \sum_\chi |W(\chi)^2|^2 \le (3P^2 + q) \sum_n |c'_n|^2$. By Cauchy--Schwarz
over the at most $F$ pairs with $jk = n$, $|c'_n|^2 \le F \sum_{jk = n} |c_j|^2 |c_k|^2$, and the sum of the right side
over $n$ is $F V^2$. This is (a). For (b), Cauchy--Schwarz over $\mathcal X$ gives $\sum_{\mathcal X} |W|^2 \le
|\mathcal
X|^{1/2} (\sum_{\mathcal X} |W|^4)^{1/2}$, and the sum over $\mathcal X$ is at most the sum over all the characters. For
(c), the map $(j, k) \mapsto j$ sends the pairs with $jk = n$ injectively to divisors of $n$, and $n \le 4P^2$ if there
is such a pair.
\end{proof}

Part (a) is orthogonality applied to the square of the polynomial, with a divisor bound for the coefficients. It is not
a fourth moment of $L$-functions: the hybrid fourth moment of $L(1/2 + it, \chi)$ is a different result, which the
density estimates behind the curves use. We call (b) the \emph{third bound} for a mean square over a bin, after the two
of Section~\ref{sec:two-bounds}, and the estimate of this subsection the estimate with the third bound. In exponents,
for a polynomial of length $q^m$ over a bin of about
$q^{g(\beta)}$ characters and with $F \le C q^{2\varepsilon'}$, (b) gives the exponent $g(\beta)/2 + \max(m, 1/2) +
\varepsilon'$ of the mean square, which Definition~\ref{def:zone4} rounds up to $g(\beta)/2 + \max(m, 1/2) +
2\varepsilon'$. For $m \le 1/2$ orthogonality exceeds $PV = q^m V$ by the factor $q^{1 - m}$, whatever the bin, and the
third bound exceeds it by the factor $q^{g(\beta)/2 + 1/2 - m + 2\varepsilon'}$: the third bound does better as soon
as $g(\beta) < 1 -
4\varepsilon' = 0.996$, but not in a bin counted trivially.

\begin{definition}\label{def:zone4}
% Lean: SieveTypeIIHM.SqExp4, SieveTypeIIHM.twoBound4, SieveTypeIIHM.ZoneOK4
In the setting of Section~\ref{sec:zone}, a number $M$ is an \emph{exponent with the third bound} of the mean square of
a polynomial of length $q^m$ over the bin if it is an exponent in the sense of Section~\ref{sec:zone}, or
\[
  M \ge \max\Bigl(m,\ \frac{g(\beta)}2 + \max\Bigl(m, \frac12\Bigr) + 2\varepsilon'\Bigr).
\]
A set $\mathcal R$ of pairs $(m_1, m_2)$ satisfies the \emph{zone condition with the third bound} for a curve $g$ at
$L$ if for every $1/2 \le \beta \le 11/12$, every $0 \le \varpi \le 4 \cdot 10^{-6}$ and every $(m_1, m_2) \in
\mathcal R$ there are exponents with the third bound $M_1$ and $M_2$ of the two mean squares, with $\delta' =
\varepsilon' = 1/1000$, such that $E \le L - 1/25000$.
\end{definition}

So an exponent with the third bound is one given by any of the three bounds, and every exponent of
Section~\ref{sec:zone} is one.

\begin{proposition}[the estimate with the third bound]\label{prop:type2-gen4}
% Lean: SieveTypeIIHM.typeII_two_gen4 (with SieveTypeIIHM.bin_bound_two4, SieveTypeIIHM.sqTerm4_le_pow, SieveTypeIIHM.sum_typeII_two_bins4)
Assume Input~\ref{inp:half-plane}. Let $g$ be a density curve with $g \le 1$ and $g(1/2) = 1$, and let $\mathcal R$
satisfy the zone condition with the third bound for $g$ at $L$. Then the conclusion of Theorem~\ref{thm:type2} holds
with $\mathcal R$ in place of $\mathcal R_L$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The proof is that of Proposition~\ref{prop:type2-gen}, with two changes: Lemma~\ref{lem:bin} gains a case, and the
pairs are counted. First, in Lemma~\ref{lem:bin}, let $C_F \ge 0$ be given with $C_0, C_d, C_K$, and suppose that for
the sets $S_i$ of its conclusion a number $0 \le F_i \le C_F q^{2\varepsilon'}$ bounds, for every $n$, the number of
pairs $(j, k) \in S_i \times S_i$ with $jk = n$. Then the lemma
holds for a set that satisfies the zone condition with the third bound, with $C' = C_0(e(2 + 12C_dC_K) +
(12C_dC_F)^{1/2})$. The new case is an exponent $M_i$ by the third bound. Since $(qT)^{g(\beta)} \le 3q^{g(\beta) +
\eta}$ and $3P_i^2 + q \le 4q^{2\max(m_i, 1/2)}$, Lemma~\ref{lem:fourth}(b) bounds the mean square by
\begin{multline*}
  (3C_d)^{1/2} (1 + \log qT)^5 q^{(g(\beta) + \eta)/2} \cdot 2C_F^{1/2} q^{\max(m_i, 1/2) + \varepsilon'} V_i \\
  \le (12C_dC_F)^{1/2} (1 + \log qT)^{10} q^{M_i + \eta} V_i,
\end{multline*}
since $M_i \ge g(\beta)/2 + \max(m_i, 1/2) + 2\varepsilon'$, and the remainder of the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:bin} is
unchanged. Second, in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:type2-gen}, take $\varepsilon = 1/(1000(|L| + 2))$ in
Lemma~\ref{lem:fourth}(c). Enlarge $q_0$ so that $q \ge 2^{K_0}$. Since the scales $Q_j$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:type2} are at least $1$, $P_1P_2 \le 2^{K_0} q^L \le q^{1 + L}$, so $0
\le m_i \le 1 + L$ and $C_\varepsilon (4P_i^2)^\varepsilon \le 4C_\varepsilon q^{2m_i\varepsilon} \le 4C_\varepsilon
q^{2/1000}$: this is $F_i \le C_F q^{2\varepsilon'}$ with $C_F = 4C_\varepsilon$. The remainder of the proof is
unchanged.
\end{proof}

\emph{The leaves' region.} At a leaf, $d_1$ is the first variable, of length $q^{m_1}$, the cofactor the second, of
length $q^{m_2}$, and $d_2$ the rest, of length $q^\rho$, with $\rho = L - m_1 - m_2$. The region below is cut out
of three ranges, chosen and not derived, by seven inequalities, the cuts. The proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:leaf-zone} takes its cases with the curve $g^*$ of Section~\ref{sec:inputs}: the trivial count
below $4/7$, then Ingham's exponent and those that Input~\ref{inp:cgl} gives up to $131/168$, and $g_{\mathrm H}$ from
there on (the paragraph \emph{Why the curve is $g^*$} below says why). The computation of \S S8 added the cuts one at
a time, each the case most violated at a vertex of the region so far, until all the cases held.

\begin{definition}\label{def:leaf-region}
% Lean: SieveTypeIIHM.LeafZone, SieveLeafTwo.LeafZoneX
Let $L$ be real. The set $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_L$ is the set of the pairs $(m_1, m_2)$ of reals such that, with
$\rho = L - m_1 - m_2$,
\[
  \frac9{10} \le m_1 \le \frac65, \qquad \frac25 \le m_2 \le \frac12, \qquad \frac3{10} \le \rho \le 1,
\]
and
\begin{align*}
  \tfrac12 m_1 + \tfrac12 m_2 + \tfrac{749993}{1750000}\rho &\ge \tfrac{25001}{25000}, \tag*{(C1)} \\
  \tfrac12 m_2 + \tfrac{749993}{1750000}\rho &\ge \tfrac{12501}{25000}, \tag*{(C2)} \\
  \tfrac12 m_1 + \tfrac12 m_2 + \tfrac{1174979}{5250000}\rho &\ge \tfrac{73693427}{84000000}, \tag*{(C3)} \\
  \tfrac12 m_2 + \tfrac{62497}{750000}\rho &\ge \tfrac{87937}{300000}, \tag*{(C4)} \\
  \tfrac12 m_1 + \tfrac12 m_2 + \tfrac{748427}{2625000}\rho &\ge \tfrac{10274479}{11200000}, \tag*{(C5)} \\
  \tfrac12 m_2 + \tfrac{748427}{2625000}\rho &\ge \tfrac{4674479}{11200000}, \tag*{(C6)} \\
  \tfrac{2753}{6000} m_1 + \tfrac12 m_2 + \tfrac{1174979}{5250000}\rho &\ge \tfrac{70467547}{84000000}. \tag*{(C7)}
\end{align*}
The \emph{leaves' region} $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ is the set of the pairs $(m_1, m_2)$ with either $m_2 \le 1/2$
and $(m_1, m_2) \in \mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_L$, or $m_2 \ge 1/2$ and $(m_1, 1/2) \in \mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_{L -
m_2 + 1/2}$.
\end{definition}

In the second case the rest is the same, $(L - m_2 + 1/2) - m_1 - 1/2 = \rho$: a cofactor longer than $\sqrt q$ is in
the region when the point with the same $m_1$ and $\rho$ and the cofactor $\sqrt q$ is.

Each cut is the case of $E \le L - 1/25000$ at $\varpi = 4 \cdot 10^{-6}$ and at one point $\beta$, with numbers $c_1$
and $c_2$ in place of the exponents $M_1$ and $M_2$ (a computation, \S S8):
\[
  \frac{m_1 - c_1}2 + \frac{m_2 - c_2}2 + \rho\bigl(1 - \beta - 4 \cdot 10^{-6}\bigr) \ge \frac1{25000}.
\]
The table below gives $\beta$ and the $c_i$, with $A$ the exponent of the piece of $g^*$ used, $g^*(\beta) = A(1 -
\beta)$. An entry $1$ or $m_1$ is the exponent $\max(m_i, 1)$ of orthogonality, $A(1 - \beta) + b(m_1)$ is that of the
Hal\'asz--Montgomery inequality, and $A(1 - \beta)/2 + 1/2 + 2\varepsilon'$ that of the third bound. Here $b(m) = 5/12
+ m/12 + \delta'(1 - m) + 2\varepsilon'$ is the exponent of the bound for $|k_{2P}(\psi)|$ at $P = q^m$
(Section~\ref{sec:two-bounds}); it gives the coefficient $2753/6000 = 1/2 - (1/12 - \delta')/2$ of $m_1$ in (C7). The
pieces of (C3), (C5), (C6) and (C7) are steps that Input~\ref{inp:cgl} gives; (C3) is at the right end of its piece,
(C7) at the left end of the next.

\begin{center}
\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lllll@{}}
\toprule
cut & $\beta$ & $A$ & $c_1$ ($d_1$) & $c_2$ (cofactor) \\
\midrule
(C1) & $4/7$ & (not used) & $1$ & $1$ \\
(C2) & $4/7$ & (not used) & $m_1$ & $1$ \\
(C3) & $163/210$ & $225661/100000$ & $1$ & $A(1 - \beta)/2 + 1/2 + 2\varepsilon'$ \\
(C4) & $11/12$ & $101/50$ & $m_1$ & $A(1 - \beta)/2 + 1/2 + 2\varepsilon'$ \\
(C5) & $1201/1680$ & $11667/5000$ & $1$ & $A(1 - \beta)/2 + 1/2 + 2\varepsilon'$ \\
(C6) & $1201/1680$ & $11667/5000$ & $m_1$ & $A(1 - \beta)/2 + 1/2 + 2\varepsilon'$ \\
(C7) & $163/210$ & $225207/100000$ & $A(1 - \beta) + b(m_1)$ & $A(1 - \beta)/2 + 1/2 + 2\varepsilon'$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:leaf-zone}
% Lean: SieveTypeIIHM.zone_leaf, SieveLeafTwo.zone_leafX (with SieveTypeIIHM.zonePiece4_OO, SieveTypeIIHM.zonePiece4_OE, SieveTypeIIHM.zonePiece4_HE, SieveTypeIIHM.zonePiece4_of_ends, SieveTypeIIHM.zonePiece4_glue, SieveLeafTwo.sqExp4_shift, SieveLeafTwo.twoBound4_shift)
For every real $L$, the sets $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_L$ and $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ satisfy the zone condition
with the third bound for $g^*$ at $L$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
With $\Delta_i = M_i - m_i$, the inequality $E \le L - 1/25000$ reads $\Delta_1 + \Delta_2 + \mu \le 2\rho(1 - \beta -
\varpi)$ with $\mu = 1/12500$, as in Section~\ref{sec:region}. On $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_L$, $\rho \ge 3/10 \ge
0$, so every case below is hardest at the largest $\varpi$: take $\varpi = 4 \cdot 10^{-6}$. The first variable takes
orthogonality, $\Delta_1 = \max(0, 1
- m_1)$, or the Hal\'asz--Montgomery inequality, $\Delta_1 = \max(0, g^*(\beta) - T_* + H_1(1 - m_1))$, with $T_* =
249/500$ and $H_1 = 11/12 + \delta'$. The cofactor,
of length at most $\sqrt q$, takes orthogonality, $\Delta_2 = 1 - m_2$, or the third bound, where $\max(m_2, 1/2) = 1/2$
and $\Delta_2 = \max(0, g^*(\beta)/2 + 1/2 + 2\varepsilon' - m_2)$.

\emph{For $1/2 \le \beta \le 4/7$}, take both exponents by orthogonality, whatever the curve. Each of the four choices
of the maxima in $M_i = \max(m_i, 1)$ is affine in $\beta$. \emph{On each of the $49$ pieces $[\beta_-, \beta_+)$ of
$g^*$ from $4/7$ to $163/210$}, where $g^*(\beta) = A(1 - \beta)$, take $d_1$ by orthogonality and the cofactor by the
third bound. \emph{On each of the $17$ pieces from $163/210$ to $11/12$}, the last one used on $[5/6, 11/12]$, take
$d_1$ by the Hal\'asz--Montgomery inequality and the cofactor by the third bound. The pieces of the second case are
those with $A(1 - \beta_+) \ge T_*$, the value of the curve below which the Hal\'asz--Montgomery inequality
does better for a variable of length $q$ (Section~\ref{sec:region}): the last of them has $A(1 - \beta_+) =
0.50505\ldots$ at $\beta_+ = 163/210$, and the first piece of the third case has $0.49599\ldots$ at $\beta_+ =
131/168$. On every
piece the exponents are affine in $\beta$, and so is each of the four choices of the maxima; so it suffices to check the
four cases at the two ends of each piece. A piece of $g^*$ is $[\beta_-, \beta_+)$; the case at $\beta_+$ is checked
with that piece's $A$, and the next piece covers $\beta_+$ itself.

At an end of a piece, a case is a linear inequality in $(m_1, m_2, \rho)$, and $L$ does not enter it. The inequalities
of Definition~\ref{def:leaf-region} define a polytope in $(m_1, m_2, \rho)$, bounded by the three ranges and
independent of $L$.
The $67 \times 2 \times 4 = 536$ cases hold at its vertices, in exact rational arithmetic (\S S8), and so on the
polytope; the seven cuts are seven of them, with no slack. In the formalisation each case
is a linear combination of the $13$ inequalities with nonnegative coefficients, for every $L$.

For $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ it remains to take $m_2 \ge 1/2$, with $(m_1, 1/2) \in \mathcal
R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_{L'}$ and $L' = L - m_2 + 1/2$. The proof above, for $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_{L'}$, gives
exponents $M_1$ and $M_2$ at $(\beta, \varpi)$ for $(m_1, 1/2)$ at $L'$. Then $M_1$ and $M_2 + (m_2 - 1/2)$ are
exponents with the third bound for $(m_1, m_2)$. Indeed $M_2 \ge 1/2$, so $M_2 + m_2 - 1/2 \ge m_2$; and, with $g =
g^*(\beta)$, if $M_2 \ge 1$ then $M_2 + m_2 - 1/2 \ge 1$; if $M_2 \ge g + b(1/2)$ then $M_2 + m_2 - 1/2 \ge g +
b(m_2)$, since $b(m_2) - b(1/2) = (m_2 - 1/2)(1/12 - \delta') \le m_2 - 1/2$; and if $M_2 \ge g/2 + 1/2 +
2\varepsilon'$ then $M_2 + m_2 - 1/2 \ge g/2 + \max(m_2, 1/2) + 2\varepsilon'$. The rest $\rho$ is the same, and both
sides of $E \le L - 1/25000$ grow by $m_2 - 1/2$ from $L'$ to $L$.
\end{proof}

At $L = 21/10$ the set $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_L$ is the quadrilateral with the vertices $(m_1, m_2, \rho) \approx
(0.98276, 0.5, 0.61724)$, $(0.98677, 0.49599, 0.61724)$, $(1.00091, 0.48396, 0.61513)$ and $(1.01299, 0.5, 0.58701)$,
cut out by $m_2 \le 1/2$, (C5), (C6) and (C7) (a computation, \S S8). In exponents of $x$, $d_1$ lies in $[x^{0.468},
x^{0.4824}]$, the cofactor in $[x^{0.2305}, x^{0.2381}]$ and $d_2$ in $[x^{0.2795}, x^{0.2939}]$. For $m_2 \ge 1/2$ the
condition on $(m_1, \rho)$ is a polygon that does not depend on $L$.

\emph{Why the curve is $g^*$.} At a $\beta$ where the curve is $1$, the cofactor costs at least $1 - m_2 \ge 1/2$: the
third bound costs $1 + 2\varepsilon' - m_2$, and the Hal\'asz--Montgomery inequality more. So the zone condition asks
$1/2 + \mu \le 2\rho(1 - \beta)$ there. With $g_{\mathrm H}$, which counts trivially up to $\beta^\dagger = 0.7789$,
that is about $\rho \ge 1.1309$, a rest longer than $q$, while $\rho \le 0.6173$ on $\mathcal
R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_{21/10}$. With $g^*$, trivial only below $4/7$, it is about $\rho \ge 0.5834$, the cut (C2) at $m_2 =
1/2$. With the trivial count up to $5/7$ it would be about $\rho \ge 0.8751$ (computations, not formalised). So the
leaves' region needs a density estimate below $5/7$; it uses Ingham's from $4/7$ and those of
Input~\ref{inp:cgl} from $5/7$, at whose pieces the cuts (C3), (C5), (C6) and (C7) lie.

\begin{theorem}[the estimate on the leaves' region]\label{thm:type2-leaves}
% Lean: SieveLeafTwo.typeIITwoAt_leafX (from SieveTypeIIHM.typeII_two_gen4, SieveLeafTwo.zone_leafX, SieveCurve7D.counts_7d, SieveCurve7D.g7d_le_one, SieveCurve7D.g7d_half)
Assume Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}. For every real $L$, the conclusion of Theorem~\ref{thm:type2}
holds with $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ in place of $\mathcal R_L$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:curve-star}, $g^*$ is a density curve under the two inputs, with $g^* \le 1$ and $g^*(1/2) =
1$. Apply Proposition~\ref{prop:type2-gen4} with the set $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:leaf-zone}.
\end{proof}

The sieve meets the leaves as sums of $S(\mathcal A_d, w)$ over tuples of primes. The next lemma is
Lemma~\ref{lem:term-two} for them, with the sifting at $w$ in place of the last prime and the $w$-rough cofactor in the
second variable. In it the first variable is $\prod_{\mathcal S} y_i$, the rest $\prod_{\mathcal S_r} y_i$, and the
second variable the integer divided by the two: the cofactor $n/d$, with $d = y_1 \cdots y_K$, and the primes of the
tuple off $\mathcal S \cup \mathcal S_r$; the tuples need neither be ordered nor have distinct entries. The sets
$\mathcal A$, $\mathcal B$ and the integers $N_1 = \lfloor q^L \rfloor$, $N_2 = \lfloor 2q^L \rfloor$ are as in
Section~\ref{sec:type1}, here for every $0 < L \le 5/2$; the comparisons are those defined there, and the condition on
$m_1 + m_2$, with its factor $4$, is that of Lemma~\ref{lem:chains-two}.

\begin{lemma}[a leaf in two variables]\label{lem:leaf-term}
% Lean: SieveLeafTwo.leavesE_of_zone, SieveLeafTwo.LeavesE, SieveLeafTwo.leaf2_bound
Let $0 < L \le 5/2$ and let $\mathcal R$ be a set of pairs for which the conclusion of Theorem~\ref{thm:type2} holds
at $L$ with $\mathcal R$ in place of $\mathcal R_L$. Fix $e_0 > 0$, integers $K_0, J_0 \ge 0$, and a real $\Lambda_1
\ge 0$. There are $\delta > 0$ and $q_0$ such that the following holds for every $q \ge q_0$, every $a$ prime to $q$
and every integer $w \ge 2$ with $w \ge q^{e_0}$. Let $P_1, P_1', R_-, R_+$ be integers with $P_1, P_1', R_+ \ge 1$,
$R_- \ge 0$ and $P_1' R_+ \le N_1$, such that $(m_1, m_2) \in \mathcal R$ for every $m_1 \in [\log_q P_1, \log_q
P_1']$ and every $m_2$ with $\lfloor N_1/R_+ \rfloor \le 4q^{m_1 + m_2}$ and $q^{m_1 + m_2} \le \lfloor N_2/(R_- + 1)
\rfloor$. Let $K \le K_0$; let $\mathcal S$ and $\mathcal S_r$ be disjoint subsets of $\{1, \dots, K\}$ with $\mathcal
S_r \ne \emptyset$; let $(y_1, \dots, y_K)$ run over the $K$-tuples of primes up to $N_2$ that satisfy at most $J_0$
comparisons with constant terms at most $\Lambda_1 \log q$; and suppose that every tuple of primes that satisfies them
has $y_i \ge w$ for every $i$, $P_1 < \prod_{\mathcal S} y_i \le P_1'$ and $R_- < \prod_{\mathcal S_r} y_i \le R_+$.
Then, with $d = y_1 \cdots y_K$,
\[
  \Bigl|\sum_{(y_1, \dots, y_K)} \Bigl(S(\mathcal A_d, w) - \frac{S(\mathcal B_d, w)}{\varphi(q)}\Bigr)\Bigr|
  \le \frac{q^{L - \delta}}{\varphi(q)}.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For one $K$ the proof is that of Lemma~\ref{lem:term-two}, with $w$ in place of the last prime $r_k$. Every prime of $d$
is at least $w$, so an integer $n$ with $d \mid n$ has no prime factor below $w$ exactly when its cofactor $n/d$ has
none. The cofactor, at most $N_2 < q^{L + 1}$, is then the product of a unique non-increasing tuple $z_1 \ge \dots \ge
z_t \ge w$ of primes, with $t \le t_1 = \lceil (L + 1)/e_0 \rceil$ since $w \ge q^{e_0}$. For each $t$, the pairs
$(\mathbf y, \mathbf z)$ are the $(K + t)$-tuples of primes up to $N_2$ that satisfy the given comparisons on $\mathbf
y$, the comparisons $z_b \le z_a$ for $a \le b$ and $z_a \ge w$, and $N_1 < \prod \mathbf y \prod \mathbf z \le N_2$: at
most $J_0 + t_1^2 + t_1 + 2$ comparisons, with constant terms at most $(\Lambda_1 + L + 1)\log q$. Apply
Lemma~\ref{lem:chains-two} to them with $\mathcal S_1 = \mathcal S$ and $\mathcal S_2$ the indices
off $\mathcal S \cup \mathcal S_r$, among them all those of $\mathbf z$; $P_1$ and $P_1'$ are those of the lemma, $P_2 =
\lfloor N_1/(P_1'R_+) \rfloor \ge 1$, $P_2' = \lfloor N_2/((P_1 + 1)(R_- + 1)) \rfloor$, and the band is that of
Lemma~\ref{lem:term-two}, with $M_{\mathrm{lo}} = \lfloor N_1/R_+ \rfloor \ge 1$. The region's hypothesis is the one
assumed. Half the least $\delta$ of the at most $t_1 + 1$ values of $t$ will do for large $q$; for all $K \le K_0$ at
once, take the least $\delta$ and the largest $q_0$ of the $K_0 + 1$ values of $K$.
\end{proof}

Section~\ref{sec:leaves-three} applies the lemma with $\mathcal R = \mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$, by
Theorem~\ref{thm:type2-leaves}, to the third range of the leaves of a comparison.

\subsection{Harman's comparisons with the leaves cut in three}\label{sec:leaves-three}

This subsection weakens hypothesis (d) of Propositions~\ref{prop:harman-two} and~\ref{prop:harman-fill}, so that the
tests (J2) and (J3) of Definition~\ref{def:tests} reach more boxes. It cuts the large leaves of Harman's comparison in
three ranges: two that Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves} evaluates, and a third that Lemma~\ref{lem:leaf-term} evaluates on the
leaves' region $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ of Definition~\ref{def:leaf-region}.

Recall that (d) is the condition of Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves} on the two blocks of a leaf. With $d_1$ the product of the
primes of $\mathcal S_1$ and, if $\iota = 1$, of the new primes, and $d_2$ the product of the others, a leaf has $d_1
\le 4q^{D_1}$ and $d_2 \le 4q^{D_2}$, and (d) asks
\[
  D_1 < L - 1, \qquad \max(D_1, 1) + D_1 + 2D_2 < 2L - 1.
\]
Only the large leaves need (d), those with $d = d_1 d_2 > q^{L - 1 - 4c'}$, for a small $c' > 0$ fixed in \S S3. For
$D_1 \ge 1$ the second condition reads $D_1 + D_2 < L - 1/2$: it asks the cofactor $n/d$ of a leaf to be longer than
about $\sqrt q$, as said at the start of this section. A box whose leaves fail (d) fails (J2) and (J3) with that
choice of $\mathcal S_1$ and $\iota$. For the large leaves whose cofactor may be shorter, the cofactor becomes a
variable of its own: Lemma~\ref{lem:leaf-term} evaluates them where their exponents lie in $\mathcal
R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$, with $d_1$ as the first variable, the cofactor as the second and $d_2$ as the rest, and its bound
has no main term. The first condition of (d), $D_1 < L - 1$, is kept, at $D_1$ and at the cut $D_1^*$ below.

The large leaves of each size (the size of a leaf is the number of primes that the comparison's tree adds to the
tuple, \S S3) are cut in three by two more comparisons, with exponents $D_1^*$ and $D_2^*$ of $q$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(T1)] the leaves with $d_1 \le q^{D_1^*}$, where Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves} applies at the exponents $(D_1^*, D_2)$ in
  place of $(D_1, D_2)$;
\item[(T2)] those with $d_1 > q^{D_1^*}$ and $d_2 \le q^{D_2^*}$, where it applies at $(D_1, D_2^*)$;
\item[(T3)] those with $d_1 > q^{D_1^*}$ and $d_2 > q^{D_2^*}$, where Lemma~\ref{lem:leaf-term} applies, with $d_1$ in
  $(\lfloor q^{D_1^*} \rfloor, \lfloor 4q^{D_1} \rfloor]$ and $d_2$ in $(\lfloor q^{D_2^*} \rfloor, \lfloor 4q^{D_2}
  \rfloor]$.
\end{enumerate}
So (d) is replaced by three conditions: Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves}'s at $(D_1^*, D_2)$ and at $(D_1, D_2^*)$, and the
region $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ on the rectangle of the third range; with them come $D_1, D_2 \ge 0$ and $D_1 + D_2
< L$ (see after the statements). The two propositions below are
Propositions~\ref{prop:harman-two} and~\ref{prop:harman-fill} with (d) so replaced, under the two inputs.

\begin{proposition}[Harman's comparison in two variables, the leaves cut in three]\label{prop:harman-two3}
% Lean: SieveTwoCompE.prop6_two3_at (SieveTwoCompE.prop6_two3); the region R^lf_L: SieveLeafTwo.LeafZoneX
Assume Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}. Let $2 < L \le 5/2$ and let $\mathcal R$ be a region on which
Theorem~\ref{thm:type2} holds at $L$, as in Section~\ref{sec:type1}. Fix data as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two} ($j$, $J_0$, $\Lambda_1$, $a_0$, the $l_i$ and $h_i$, $\mathcal S_0$, $\mathcal S_r$,
$\mathcal S_1$, $\iota$, $t$, $u$, $v$, $\rho_1$, $\rho_2$, $\varepsilon$ and $\eta$), with the same conditions and with
its hypotheses (a), (b) and (c), and
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(d$_3$)] with $D_1 = \sum_{\mathcal S_1} h_i + \mathbf 1[\iota = 1]\, t$ and $D_2 = \sum_{i \notin \mathcal S_1}
  h_i + \mathbf 1[\iota = 2]\, t$: $D_1 \ge 0$, $D_2 \ge 0$, $D_1 + D_2 < L$, and there are reals $D_1^* \ge 0$, $D_2^*
  \ge 0$ and $\varepsilon_E > 0$ with
  \begin{gather*}
    D_1^* < L - 1, \qquad \max(D_1^*, 1) + D_1^* + 2D_2 < 2L - 1, \\
    D_1 < L - 1, \qquad \max(D_1, 1) + D_1 + 2D_2^* < 2L - 1,
  \end{gather*}
  and $(m_1, m_2) \in \mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ whenever $D_1^* - \varepsilon_E \le m_1 \le D_1 + \varepsilon_E$ and
  $L - D_2 - \varepsilon_E \le m_1 + m_2 \le L - D_2^* + \varepsilon_E$.
\end{enumerate}
Then the conclusion of Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two} holds.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Harman's comparison with the rest among the comparison's own small primes, the leaves cut in
three]\label{prop:harman-fill3}
% Lean: SieveFillCompE.prop6_fill3_at (SieveFillCompE.prop6_fill3)
Assume Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}. Let $L$ and $\mathcal R$ be as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two3}. Fix data as in Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-fill} ($j$, $J_0$, $\Lambda_1$,
$a_0$, the $l_i$ and $h_i$, $\mathcal S_0$, $\mathcal S_1$, $\iota$, $t$, $u$, $v$, $\tau$, $\rho_2$, $\varepsilon$ and
$\eta$), with the same conditions and with its hypotheses (a), (b) and (c), and (d$_3$) of
Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two3} with
\[
  D_1 = \sum_{\mathcal S_1} h_i + \mathbf 1[\iota = 1](t + \rho_2), \qquad D_2 = \sum_{i \notin \mathcal S_1} h_i +
  \mathbf 1[\iota = 2](t + \rho_2).
\]
Then the conclusion of Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two} holds. For $j = 0$ the sum is the single term $S(\mathcal A,
z_0) - S(\mathcal B, z_0)/\varphi(q)$.
\end{proposition}

In (d$_3$) the first line is Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves}'s condition for the first range, with $D_1^*$ in place of $D_1$,
and the second for the second range, with $D_2^*$ in place of $D_2$. In the region's condition $m_1$ is the exponent of
$d_1$, $m_2$ that of the cofactor and $L - m_1 - m_2$ that of $d_2$: the rectangle is that of
Lemma~\ref{lem:leaf-term} on the third range, with the margin $\varepsilon_E$ for the integer parts and
the factors $4$. The condition $D_1 + D_2 < L$ makes the product of the upper ends of the ranges of $d_1$ and $d_2$ at
most $x$, as
Lemma~\ref{lem:leaf-term} asks; (d) implies it, (d$_3$) has to say it. The two inputs enter through the estimate on
$\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:type2-leaves}). When $D_1, D_2 \ge 0$, (d) implies (d$_3$): take $D_2^* =
0$, a small $\varepsilon_E$, and $D_1^*$ slightly above $D_1 + 2\varepsilon_E$, so that the rectangle of the third
range is empty. Under the two inputs, Propositions~\ref{prop:harman-two3} and~\ref{prop:harman-fill3} therefore extend
Propositions~\ref{prop:harman-two} and~\ref{prop:harman-fill}.

\begin{proof}
The proof is that of Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two} (\S S3) with the large leaves of each size cut in three; \S S8
gives the details and the constants. The stops, and the tuples whose first variable is already in its range, are
evaluated by Lemma~\ref{lem:term-two} with the hypotheses (a), (b) and (c), as there. The small leaves, $d \le q^{L - 1
- 4c'}$, are counted by the bound of the leaves in \S S3. The large leaves of one size form a family of decreasing
tuples of primes in $[w, N_2]$, with $d_1 \le 4q^{D_1}$ and $d_2 \le 4q^{D_2}$, which the two comparisons $d_1 \le
q^{D_1^*}$ and $d_2 \le q^{D_2^*}$ cut into the three ranges (T1), (T2) and (T3). When neither block is empty, each of
the two comparisons has a pivot, as Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves} asks. When $d_1 = 1$ or $d_2 = 1$ no cut is needed: all the
large leaves of the size satisfy the conditions of Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves} at $(D_1^*, D_2)$, or at $(D_1, D_2^*)$,
respectively.

On (T1) and (T2), Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves} applies at the exponents $(D_1^*, D_2)$ and $(D_1, D_2^*)$, with the choices
that the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two} makes for $(D_1, D_2)$, and with $c' = \min(1, \omega_*)/32$, where
$\omega_* > 0$ is the least of $L - 1 - \max(D_1^*, 1)$, $2L - 1 - \max(D_1^*, 1) - D_1^* - 2D_2$, $L - 1 - \max(D_1,
1)$ and $2L - 1 - \max(D_1, 1) - D_1 - 2D_2^*$, positive by (d$_3$) and $L > 2$. On each of the two ranges its bound is
the main term of Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves}, with the sum of $1/d$ over the range, and an error $q^{L -
\delta_3}/\varphi(q)$, with $\delta_3 > 0$
fixed in \S S8. On (T3), Lemma~\ref{lem:leaf-term}, with $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ as its region by
Theorem~\ref{thm:type2-leaves}, $d_1$ as the first variable and $d_2$ as the rest, bounds the whole sum by $q^{L -
\delta_3}/\varphi(q)$: there is no main term, since the lemma bounds the difference over $\mathcal A$ and $\mathcal
B/\varphi(q)$ itself. Its hypotheses are (d$_3$): $D_1 + D_2 < L$ gives $\lfloor 4q^{D_1} \rfloor \lfloor 4q^{D_2}
\rfloor \le N_1$ for large $q$, and the region's condition gives, for large $q$, the region on the rectangle that the
lemma asks for.

The sums of $1/d$ over (T1) and over (T2) add up to at most the sum over the whole size, since the ranges are
disjoint. So the main terms of the size are at most those of the uncut size, and their total over the sizes is
bounded as in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two}, by the same choice of the parameter $k$ of Brun's sieve.
Each size has three errors $q^{L - \delta_3}/\varphi(q)$ in place of one, one for each range, and there are boundedly
many sizes, so the cut changes only the final $\delta$. For Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-fill3} the same cut is made at
each size of the leaves of each of the comparison's trees in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-fill}, with the
growth $t + \rho_2$ in $D_1$ and $D_2$.
\end{proof}

\emph{The tests.} The condition (d$_3$) becomes a condition on a box's exponents in units of $1/D$, as (d) became
(L). For integers $A, B \ge 0$, write (L) at $(A, B)$ for the leaves' condition (L) of Definition~\ref{def:tests} with
$a_1 = A/D$ and $a_2 = B/D$. For reals $m_1$, $m_2$ and $\rho$, let $(\mathrm Z)(m_1, m_2, \rho)$ be the cuts (C1) to
(C7) of Definition~\ref{def:leaf-region}, with $\rho$ taken as a variable of its own in place of $L - m_1 - m_2$. The
region $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$ is tested through nineteen inequalities that imply it: five bounds on $m_1$, $m_2$
and $\rho$, and the seven cuts (Z) at two points.

\begin{definition}\label{def:leaves3}
% Lean: SieveAd.leavesE, SieveAd.leavesL, SieveAd.pStar, SieveAd.thetaL, SieveLeafZoneX.leafXR, SieveLeafZoneX.LeafZoneXL
Let $A, B \ge 0$ be integers. The \emph{leaves cut in three} satisfy the condition $(\mathrm L_3)$ at $(A, B)$ if there
are integers $A^* \ge 1$ with (L) at $(A^*, B)$ and $B^* \ge 1$ with (L) at $(A, B^*)$, and, with $A^*$ and $B^*$ the
largest such,
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] $A + B < D$;
\item[(ii)] each of the nineteen inequalities
  \begin{gather*}
    \tfrac9{10} \le m_1 \le \tfrac65, \qquad m_2 \ge \tfrac25, \qquad \tfrac3{10} \le \rho \le 1, \\
    (\mathrm Z)(m_1, m_2, \rho), \qquad (\mathrm Z)(m_1, \tfrac12, \rho),
  \end{gather*}
  with $m_2 = L - m_1 - \rho$, holds at the corner of the rectangle $[L(A^* - 1)/D, L(A + 1)/D] \times [L(B^* - 1)/D,
  L(B + 1)/D]$ of the pairs $(m_1, \rho)$ where it is tightest.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

So $A^*$ is the cut of $d_1$ and $B^*$ that of $d_2$, in units of $1/D$ of exponents of $x$; Lemma~\ref{lem:tests3}
applies (d$_3$) with $D_1 = LA/D$, $D_2 = LB/D$, $D_1^* = LA^*/D$, $D_2^* = LB^*/D$ and $\varepsilon_E = L/(2D)$. The
rectangle is that of the third range, $d_1$ from about $x^{A^*/D}$ to $x^{A/D}$ and $d_2$ from about $x^{B^*/D}$ to
$x^{B/D}$, with one unit of margin on each side. So, up to the margins, (L$_3$) asks (L) at $(A^*, B)$ and at $(A,
B^*)$, $A + B < D$, and the leaves' region on the third range; in powers of $q$, the region's first five inequalities
ask $d_1$ to lie between about $q^{9/10}$ and $q^{6/5}$, $d_2$ between about $q^{3/10}$ and $q$, and the cofactor to be
at least about $q^{2/5}$.

Each of the nineteen inequalities is affine in $(m_1, \rho)$, so it holds on the whole rectangle when it holds at the
corner of (ii) where it is tightest. On the rectangle they imply $(m_1, m_2) \in \mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$. If $m_2
\le 1/2$, the inequalities of $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_L$ other than $m_2 \le 1/2$, which holds, are among them. If
$m_2 \ge 1/2$, those of $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf},0}_{L - m_2 + 1/2}$ at $(m_1, 1/2)$, with the
same rest $\rho$, are among them, except the bounds $2/5 \le 1/2 \le 1/2$, which hold; this is the other half of
Definition~\ref{def:leaf-region}. The largest $A^*$ and
$B^*$ are explicit: (L) at $(A^*, B)$ is
three
upper bounds for $A^*$, and (L) at $(A, B^*)$ is a condition on $A$ and two upper bounds for $B^*$. For rational $L$
every condition, with the integer parts of the bounds for $A^*$ and $B^*$, is an inequality between integers, and is
checked exactly.
% Lean: SieveLeafZoneX.leafXR_sound, SieveLeafZoneX.leafZoneX_of_XL, SieveAd.leavesE_cuts, SieveAd.leavesL_real

Among the tests of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}, besides those of the window (Section~\ref{sec:window-tests}), are those of
Definition~\ref{def:tests} and of Section~\ref{sec:reversal} with the leaves' condition relaxed. For a test (J2) or
(J3) with the growth $g$ (the integer of Definition~\ref{def:tests}), let
\[
  A = h(\mathcal S_1) + \mathbf 1[\iota = 1]\, g, \qquad B = h(\mathfrak b) - h(\mathcal S_1) + \mathbf 1[\iota = 2]\, g
\]
be the numerators of $a_1$ and $a_2$; over a frozen part, $h(\mathcal S_1)$ and $h(\mathfrak b) - h(\mathcal S_1)$ are
replaced by $\overline e(\mathcal S_1) + \gamma$ and $\overline e(\overline{\mathcal S_1}) + \gamma$, with the room
$\gamma$ of Section~\ref{sec:reversal}. A box passes (J2) or (J3) \emph{with (L) or (L$_3$)} at a floor $f$ if it
passes the test of Definition~\ref{def:tests} at $f$ with the condition ``(L) holds, or (L$_3$) holds at $(A, B)$'' in
place of ``(L) holds''; (J2$_{\mathcal M}$) and (J3$_{\mathcal M}$) with (L) or (L$_3$) are read in the same way.
Theorem~\ref{thm:4320} keeps
the tests of Definition~\ref{def:tests} with (L) alone; the relaxed tests enter only the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:tests3}
% Lean: SieveAd.fillTests_sound, SieveAd.evJ_twoE, SieveAd.evJ_fillE, SieveAd.evJ_two_core3, SieveAd.evJ_fill_core3,
% SieveAd.evJ_two_core, SieveAd.evJ_fill_core; over a frozen part: SieveAd.frozTests_sound, SieveAd.evJ_fam_two,
% SieveAd.evJ_fam_fill; the tests: SieveAd.chkJ2, SieveAd.chkJ3, SieveAd.okJ2, SieveAd.okJ3, SieveAd.fillTests,
% SieveAd.coreJ2, SieveAd.coreJ3, SieveAd.okJF2, SieveAd.okJF3, SieveAd.frozTests
Assume Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] The terms at the floor $f$ of a box that passes (J2) or (J3) with (L) or (L$_3$) at $f$ are evaluated.
\item[(b)] For every $J$, (a) holds uniformly when the family is cut by at most $J$ further comparisons of the kinds of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(c): $q_0$ does not depend on them. This holds also for boxes of more than $13$ primes that
  satisfy the conditions of (J2) or (J3) with (L) or (L$_3$).
\item[(c)] Let $\mathcal M$ be the wide part of a box of $k \ge 2$ primes with $0 < l_1 \le h_1 \le D$. If a chain
  $\mathfrak c$ of $j \ge 0$ primes passes (J2$_{\mathcal M}$) or (J3$_{\mathcal M}$) with (L) or (L$_3$) at $f$, then
  $\Delta_{\mathcal M}(\mathfrak c; f) = o(X)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Where (L) holds, these are Lemmas~\ref{lem:tests}(b), (c) and~\ref{lem:frozen-tests}. Let (L$_3$) hold at $(A, B)$, with
$A^*$ and $B^*$ its cuts. (a) For (J2), apply Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two3} with the region $\mathcal R$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:type2} and all the choices of the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(b), and with $D_1^* = LA^*/D$, $D_2^*
= LB^*/D$ and $\varepsilon_E = L/(2D)$. The proposition's hypotheses (a), (b) and (c) were checked there. For (d$_3$),
$D_1 = LA/D$ and
$D_2 = LB/D$ are nonnegative, and $D_1 + D_2 < L$ by (i). The first line of (d$_3$) is (L) at $(A^*, B)$, multiplied by
$L$: its first inequality is $D_1^* < L - 1$, and $\max(D_1^*, 1) + D_1^* + 2D_2 < 2L - 1$ is its second if $D_1^* \ge
1$
and its third if $D_1^* \le 1$, as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(b); the second line is (L) at $(A, B^*)$ in the
same way. For the region, let $D_1^* - \varepsilon_E \le m_1 \le D_1 + \varepsilon_E$ and $L - D_2 - \varepsilon_E \le
m_1 + m_2 \le L - D_2^* + \varepsilon_E$. Then $m_1 \in [L(A^* - 1)/D, L(A + 1)/D]$ and $\rho = L - m_1 - m_2 \in [L(B^*
- 1)/D, L(B + 1)/D]$, so by (ii) the nineteen inequalities hold at $(m_1, \rho)$, and $(m_1, m_2) \in \mathcal
R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$, as said after Definition~\ref{def:leaves3}. The proposition's bound is at most $\eta X$ for large
$q$, as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(b). For (J3), apply Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-fill3} in the same way,
with $\tau = Lr_1/D$ and $\rho_2 = Lr_2/D$; its $D_1$ and $D_2$, with the growth $t + \rho_2 = Lg/D$, are again $LA/D$
and $LB/D$.

(b) The propositions hold for every family of comparisons with the stated bounds, with $\delta$ and $q_0$ that do not
depend on them, and for chains of any length; the argument is that of Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(c).

(c) The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tests} writes $\Delta_{\mathcal M}(\mathfrak c; f)$, up to $o(X)$, as a sum of
finitely many terms at the floor $f$ of boxes of cells of one unit, under the family's comparisons, and evaluates each
by Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two} or~\ref{prop:harman-fill}, with the box's sums bounded by the frozen part's
exponents widened by the room $\gamma$. Where (L$_3$) holds, Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two3}
or~\ref{prop:harman-fill3}
evaluates each instead, with the cuts $A^*$ and $B^*$ of (L$_3$) at those bounds: the box's $D_1$ and $D_2$ are at most
$LA/D$ and $LB/D$, and the conditions of (d$_3$) only weaken as $D_1$ and $D_2$ decrease, since the cuts $A^*$ and
$B^*$ are kept and the rectangle of the region shrinks into the rectangle of (ii). By (b) this holds uniformly in the
family's comparisons, and the sum over the finitely many boxes is $o(X)$.
\end{proof}

The condition ``(L) or (L$_3$)'' is weaker than (L), so a box that passes a test of Definition~\ref{def:tests} passes
it with (L) or (L$_3$).

\subsection{The reversal of a term of the sign \texorpdfstring{$+$}{+}}\label{sec:reversal-plus}

A term with the sign $+$ needs a lower bound; role reversal is used for such terms, which would otherwise be discarded,
in the sieves of \cite{bhp} and \cite{li-harman}. A box of the sign $+$ that no test evaluates is dropped at its full
share
$c_D(\mathfrak b)/\mathcal K$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:drop}(1)), or decomposed by Buchstab's identity into a term at a floor and
boxes of the sign $-$, which need upper bounds. The reversal of Section~\ref{sec:reversal} applies to it with the roles
of $\mathcal A$ and $\mathcal B$ exchanged: the lower inequality of Lemma~\ref{lem:sandwich} goes to $\mathcal A$ and
the upper one to $\mathcal B$. The term is then bounded from below by the terms over the narrow part
$\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$ at the level $\nu$ of the reversal, less the ends, and Buchstab's identity between the floor
and the level asks for an upper
bound of the chain $((f, \nu))$ over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$: a tree with the sign $-$.

\emph{Frozen parts other than the wide one.} Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tests}, Definition~\ref{def:frozen-tree} and
Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tree} are stated for the wide part. Their proofs use of the frozen part only its bounds $e_\pm$,
in the tests, and the range $R \in (x^{(D - h_1)/D}, x^{(D - l_1 + 1)/D}]$, in the nodes (V$_{\mathcal M}$) and
(D$_{\mathcal M}$) and the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tree}. So Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tests} holds, with the same
proof and the same room, for every frozen part $\mathcal M(e_-, e_+)$ with $e_- \le e_+ \le D$, and the nodes of
Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tree} for every family of pairs whose $R$ lie in that range. We use them for the narrow part
$\mathcal M_{\mathrm n} = \mathcal M(D - h_1 + 1, D - l_1)$, which lies in that range: its tests are read with $(e_-,
e_+) = (D - h_1 + 1, D - l_1)$, and (V$_{\mathcal M}$) and (D$_{\mathcal M}$) keep their conditions and costs, in terms
of $l_1$ and $h_1$. A tree over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$ is meant in this sense; its cost stands for its multiple of
$\operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M_{\mathrm n})/\mathcal K$. The definition of $\mathcal M(e_-, e_+)$ is read with $e_- \le
e_+$: when $h_1 = l_1 + 1$ the narrow part is $\mathcal M(D - l_1, D - l_1)$, which is empty, with its terms and weight
$0$.
% Lean: SieveAd.frozTests_sound, SieveAd.frozRules, SieveAd.inSlab_frozS_inner, SieveAd.InSlab

\begin{definition}\label{def:reversal-plus}
% Lean: SieveAd.flipOK, SieveAd.flipT, SieveAd.flipTreeD, SieveAd.flipCostB, SieveAd.flipTreeDYH, SieveAd.flipCostBYH, SieveAd.flipCostDYH, SieveAd.altFDYH, SieveAd.bdK
Let $\mathfrak b$ be a box that may be reversed (Definition~\ref{def:reversal}), and $\nu = \lceil h_1/2 \rceil$. A
\emph{reversal of $\mathfrak b$ with the sign $+$} is
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] a floor $f$ with $0 < f \le \min(l_k, \nu)$ at which the chain of no prime passes (J2$_{\mathcal M}$) or
  (J3$_{\mathcal M}$) over the narrow part $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$, with the room of Section~\ref{sec:reversal}, and
\item[(ii)] a tree over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$ on the chain $((f, \nu))$ with the sign $-$, whose tests are read
  over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$; at $21/10$ its nodes (E$_{\mathcal M}$) may also use the tests (W$_{\mathcal M}$) and
  (W$'_{\mathcal M}$) of Definition~\ref{def:window-tests} over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$.
\end{enumerate}
If that tree's cost is $c_F$, the reversal's cost is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cRplus}
  c_R^+ = \Bigl\lceil \frac{c_F\, h_1 c_D(\mathfrak b) + c_e E}{D\mathcal K} \Bigr\rceil,
