\end{equation}
with $E$, $\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{lo}}$, $\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{hi}}$ and $c_e$ as in (\ref{eq:cR}).
\end{definition}

\emph{What changes with the sign.} The conditions on the box are those of a reversal with the sign $-$, and a tree over
a frozen part is the same kind of tree for both signs, with the same nodes and the same tests read over its part; at
$21/10$ both take the changes of Definition~\ref{def:tree-b}. Three things change. First, the frozen part is wide for
$-$ and narrow for $+$: for the sign $+$ the tree runs over the narrow part only, from the side of $\mathcal A$, and the
ends come in only from the side of $\mathcal B$, through the upper inequality of Lemma~\ref{lem:sandwich}, at the cost
$c_e$ of the ends (Lemma~\ref{lem:rough}). Second, the factor of $c_F$ in the cost is therefore $h_1 c_D(\mathfrak b)$
in (\ref{eq:cRplus}), not $h_1 c_D(\mathfrak b) + E$ as in (\ref{eq:cR}). Third, and this is the price, the chain
$((f, \nu))$ has the sign $-$, not $+$. As a node (D$_{\mathcal M}$) asks the sign $+$, the tree cannot drop the chain
of one prime or its cuts: each is empty, evaluated, cut again, or decomposed by Buchstab's identity into chains of two
primes, which have the sign $+$ and may be dropped.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:reversal-plus}
% Lean: SieveAd.bd_flip_pos, SieveAd.bd_flip_core_pos, SieveAd.bd_flip_coreB, SieveAd.bd_flipCostDYH, SieveAd.altFDYH_sound, SieveAd.froz_top_ge, SieveAd.froz_ends_le, SieveAd.dJF_level, SieveAd.sum_frozS_three, SieveAd.bd_of_eps, SieveAd.rules_frozResYH, SieveAd.evIF_froz_resTY
Let $\mathfrak b$ be a box of $k \ge 2$ primes and $\nu = \lceil h_1/2 \rceil$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] Let $0 < l_1 < h_1 \le D$, $\nu \le l_1$, $h_1 + \nu + 1 \le D$ and $h_2 \le l_1$. Let $0 \le f \le \nu$ be
  an integer, $C \ge 0$, $C_e \ge \Omega[(l_1 - 1)/(\nu + 1), (h_1 + 2)/\nu)\, D/(\nu\mathcal K)$, $w_0$ real and $w_1,
  w_2 \ge 0$. Suppose that
  \begin{enumerate}
  \item[(i)] $\Delta_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}}(\emptyset; f) = o(X)$;
  \item[(ii)] for every $\eta > 0$, for large $q$ and every $a$ prime to $q$, $-\Delta_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}}(((f,
    \nu))) \ge -(C \operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}) + \eta) X$;
  \item[(iii)] for every $\delta > 0$ and large $q$, $\operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}) \le w_0 + \delta$,
    $\operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M(D - h_1, D - h_1 + 1)) \le w_1 + \delta$ and $\operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M(D - l_1,
    D - l_1 + 1)) \le w_2 + \delta$.
  \end{enumerate}
  Then the term of $\mathfrak b$ with the sign $+$ is bounded at $C w_0 + C_e(w_1 + w_2)$.
\item[(b)] If $\mathfrak b$ may be reversed, with a reversal with the sign $+$ of cost $c_R^+$ whose tree passes, then
  the term of $\mathfrak b$ with the sign $+$ is bounded at $c_R^+/\mathcal K$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

The proof of (a) is that of Proposition~\ref{prop:reversal} with the inequalities of Lemma~\ref{lem:sandwich}
exchanged between the two sets; (b) checks the hypotheses of (a) from the reversal.

\begin{proof}
(a) Since $h_1 > 0$, $\nu > 0$ and $h_1 \le 2\nu$. Write $\mathcal M_{\mathrm e}$ for the union of the two ends. Since
$l_1 < h_1$, $D - h_1 + 1 \le D - l_1$, and the wide part is the disjoint union of the lower end, the narrow part and
the upper end. By Lemma~\ref{lem:sandwich}, applied to $\mathcal A$ for the lower inequality, which uses $\nu \le l_1$,
$h_1 \le 2\nu$ and $h_2 \le l_1$, and to $\mathcal B$ for the upper,
\[
  \Delta(\mathfrak b) \ge T_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}}(\mathcal A, \emptyset; \nu) - \frac{T_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm
  w}}(\mathcal B, \emptyset; \nu)}{\varphi(q)} = \Delta_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}}(\emptyset; \nu) - \frac{T_{\mathcal
  M_{\mathrm e}}(\mathcal B, \emptyset; \nu)}{\varphi(q)}.
\]

By (\ref{eq:levels}) over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$, with $f \le \nu \le l_1 < h_1 \le D$, $\Delta_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm
n}}(\emptyset; \nu) = \Delta_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}}(\emptyset; f) - \Delta_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}}(((f, \nu)))$. By
(i) the first term is at least $-\eta X$ for large $q$, and by (ii) the second is at least $-(C
\operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}) + \eta) X$. The ends lie in the wide range. So, as in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:reversal}, for $R$ in them $y = x/R \in [x^{(l_1 - 1)/D}, x^{h_1/D})$, and the integers of
$\mathcal B$ counted for $R$ at the level $\nu$ are at most $\Phi(y, z_\nu)$. The arguments $\log 2y/\log z_\nu$ lie in
$[(l_1 - 1)/(\nu + 1), (h_1 + 2)/\nu)$, and Lemma~\ref{lem:rough} gives $T_{\mathcal M_{\mathrm e}}(\mathcal B,
\emptyset; \nu)/\varphi(q) \le (C_e + \eta) \operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M_{\mathrm e}) X$ for large $q$; this bound on
the ends uses $h_1 + \nu + 1 \le D$. Hypothesis (iii) gives $\operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}) \le w_0 +
\eta$ and $\operatorname{wt}(\mathcal M_{\mathrm e}) \le w_1 + w_2 + 2\eta$. As $C \ge 0$, collecting gives
$\Delta(\mathfrak b) \ge -(C w_0 + C_e(w_1 + w_2) + O(\eta)) X$, with an $O$ that depends only on $C$, $C_e$ and the
$w_i$.

(b) Let $C = c_F/\mathcal K$, $C_e = c_e/\mathcal K$, $w_0 = (h_1/D)\, c_D(\mathfrak b)/\mathcal K$, $w_1 = ((h_1 +
1)/D)\, c_D(\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{lo}})/\mathcal K$ and $w_2 = ((l_1 + 1)/D)\, c_D(\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{hi}})/\mathcal
K$, so that $C w_0 + C_e(w_1 + w_2) = (c_F h_1 c_D(\mathfrak b) + c_e E)/(D\mathcal K^2) \le c_R^+/\mathcal K$. A box
that may be reversed has $0 < l_1 < h_1 \le D$, $\nu \le l_1$, $h_1 + \nu + 1 \le D$ and $h_2 \le l_1 - 1$, and $C_e$
satisfies the condition of (a) by the definition of $c_e$.

The chain of no prime passes (J2$_{\mathcal M}$) or (J3$_{\mathcal M}$) at $f$ over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$, and $f
\le l_k$, which the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tests} uses; so hypothesis (i) of (a) holds by
Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tests} for
$\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$. For hypothesis (ii), Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tree} for $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$ applies with
the sign
$-$, the nodes (E$_{\mathcal M}$) that pass (W$_{\mathcal M}$) or (W$'_{\mathcal M}$) over $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$
being evaluated by Lemma~\ref{lem:window-tests}(b) for $\mathcal M_{\mathrm n}$; for every $\eta' > 0$ it gives (ii)
with $C + \eta'$ in place of $C$. For hypothesis (iii), the ends are the narrow parts of $\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{lo}}$ and
$\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{hi}}$, and Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-weight} applies to $\mathfrak b$, as $h_1 \le 2\nu \le 2l_1$,
and to $\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{lo}}$ and $\mathfrak b^{\mathrm{hi}}$, exactly as in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:reversal}; it gives (iii) with $w_0$, $w_1$, $w_2$. So (a) bounds the term at
$(C + \eta') w_0 + C_e(w_1 + w_2)$ for every $\eta' > 0$, and, as $w_0 \ge 0$, at $C w_0 + C_e(w_1 + w_2) \le
c_R^+/\mathcal K$.
\end{proof}

\emph{Which boxes the search reverses.} The search at $21/10$ (Section~\ref{sec:certificate-b}) reverses boxes of either
sign, of at most six primes: the bound $11$ on $k$ of Definition~\ref{def:reversal} is lowered to $6$ there. Its main
tree is the tree on the boxes, as against the trees over frozen parts. There the root is one prime with the sign $-$, a
node (B) adds one prime and changes the sign, and a node (S) keeps both. So a box of $k$ primes has the sign $(-1)^k$:
the reversed boxes of the sign $-$ have three or five primes, those of the sign $+$ two, four or six. This is a property
of the search's plan, not a theorem: the soundness of the reversal holds for either sign. Reversals are not nested: a
tree over a frozen part reverses no box, with either sign.

\section{The certificate at \texorpdfstring{$21/10$}{21/10}, and the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}}\label{sec:certificate-b}

This section puts the rules of Sections~\ref{sec:window} and~\ref{sec:rules} into the trees of
Section~\ref{sec:decomp}, describes the search that Lean's kernel follows at $L = 21/10$ and its result, and proves
Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}. The proof uses Definition~\ref{def:tree-b} with the earlier results that extend to it,
Lemma~\ref{lem:search-b} and Proposition~\ref{prop:certificate-b}; the strategy and the hints need no proof, and the
counts of the computation are not used. The notation of Sections~\ref{sec:decomp} and~\ref{sec:certificate} is kept.

\subsection{The trees of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}}

\begin{definition}\label{def:tree-b}
% Lean: SieveAd.Rules, SieveAd.opsMP, SieveAd.resTestsFYH, SieveAd.fillTests, SieveAd.leavesE; the reversal of either sign: SieveAd.flipOKP, SieveAd.flipTreeDYH, SieveAd.flipCostBYH, SieveAd.flipCostDYH, SieveAd.altFDYH; over a frozen part: SieveAd.opsKP, SieveAd.frozResTestsYH
Let $\mathfrak b$ be a box of $k \ge 1$ primes and $\varepsilon$ a sign. A \emph{tree of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}} on
$\mathfrak b$ with the sign $\varepsilon$ is a tree of Definition~\ref{def:tree} with the following three changes; the
trees over frozen parts of Definition~\ref{def:frozen-tree} in its reversals change as said after the list.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)] A node (E) passes if $\mathfrak b$ passes (I), or (W) or (W$'$) of Definition~\ref{def:window-tests}, with
  or without the cofactor. A node (E$_{\mathcal M}$) of a tree over a frozen part passes if its chain passes
  (I$_{\mathcal M}$), or (W$_{\mathcal M}$) or (W$'_{\mathcal M}$), with or without the chain's cofactor. Cost $0$.
\item[(ii)] The tests (J2) and (J3) of a node (B), and (J2$_{\mathcal M}$) and (J3$_{\mathcal M}$) at the floor of
  a reversal and at the nodes (B) over a frozen part, are taken with (L) or (L$_3$) (Definition~\ref{def:leaves3}, and
  the paragraph before Lemma~\ref{lem:tests3}).
\item[(iii)] A new node (R$^+$), \emph{reversed with the sign $+$}: $\varepsilon = +$, and a reversal of $\mathfrak b$
  with the sign $+$ (Definition~\ref{def:reversal-plus}). Cost $c_R^+$ of (\ref{eq:cRplus}).
\end{enumerate}
The trees over the frozen parts of the reversals of either sign, over the wide part for (R) and over the narrow part
for (R$^+$), have the nodes of Definition~\ref{def:frozen-tree} with the changes (i) and (ii).
\end{definition}

Table~\ref{tab:rules} lists the rules of these trees, with what each rule needs, the statement that proves it sound
and the theorems that use it. The cost of such a tree is, as before, the sum of the costs of its dropped boxes and of
its reversals: the boxes
evaluated by the window cost nothing, like those evaluated by (I). A box of the sign $+$ may now be dropped, decomposed,
cut, evaluated or reversed, and so may a box of the sign $-$ but for the drop.

Under Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}, four earlier results extend, with their proofs, as follows.
\begin{itemize}
\item Proposition~\ref{prop:tree-bound} holds for the trees of Definition~\ref{def:tree-b}, with three more cases: a
  node (E) by (W) or (W$'$) is Lemma~\ref{lem:window-tests}(a); a node (B) whose test passes with (L$_3$) uses
  Lemma~\ref{lem:tests3}(a) in place of Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(b); and a node (R$^+$) is
  Proposition~\ref{prop:reversal-plus}(b).
\item Lemma~\ref{lem:tests3}(c) holds for every frozen part $\mathcal M(e_-, e_+)$ with $e_- \le e_+ \le D$, in
  particular the narrow part; it replaces Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tests} at the floor of a reversal of either sign, in
  Propositions~\ref{prop:reversal} and~\ref{prop:reversal-plus}(b), and at the nodes (B) of the trees over the frozen
  parts.
\item Lemma~\ref{lem:frozen-tree} holds for the trees over the frozen parts of Definition~\ref{def:tree-b}, the wide
  and the narrow ones, with
  Lemma~\ref{lem:window-tests}(b) for the nodes (E$_{\mathcal M}$) and Lemma~\ref{lem:tests3}(c) for the tests at a
  floor.
\item Theorem~\ref{thm:tree} holds with the root's test (J3) taken with (L) or (L$_3$), by Lemma~\ref{lem:tests3}(a) in
  place of Lemma~\ref{lem:tests}(b).
\end{itemize}

In the formalisation the trees of Definition~\ref{def:tree-b} are not a type: their
end nodes in the main tree, the nodes (V), (E) and the drops, are one-node trees of Definition~\ref{def:tree}, with
the tests of the search, bounded by Proposition~\ref{prop:tree-bound} (\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak sound}, through
\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak pr\_of\_has}); their other nodes are the rules of the search, which the kernel follows, and
the induction is that of the search (Lemma~\ref{lem:search-b}). The rules are
\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak rulesMP} with the soundness of the tests, \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak resTestsFYH\_sound}, for
the nodes other than the reversals; \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak bd\_flipCostDYH} for the reversals of either sign; and
\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak rules\_frozResYH} for the nodes over a frozen part. Theorem~\ref{thm:tree} is
\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak linnik\_pr} for the prime and \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak count\_large\_pr} for the count, both
of which take any sound tests: at $21/10$ the tests (I), (J2) and (J3) with (L) or
(L$_3$), sound by \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak fillTests\_sound}.

\subsection{The search and its soundness}

The search is that of Section~\ref{sec:certificate}, led by hints, with the nodes of Definition~\ref{def:tree-b}. Its
tests are the conditions of Definitions~\ref{def:tests} and~\ref{def:frozen-tree} with (L) or (L$_3$), in integers, and
the kernel's tests of (W) and (W$'$) of Section~\ref{sec:window-tests}, led by the hints; its
costs are (\ref{eq:cD}), $c'_D$, (\ref{eq:cR}) and (\ref{eq:cRplus}). A reversal of either sign is searched as in
Section~\ref{sec:certificate}: the test at its floor over the wide or the narrow part, then the tree over that part on
$((f, \nu))$, with the sign opposite to the box's, searched with the fixed limit $64\mathcal K$. Its soundness is proved
once, for every hint.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:search-b}
% Lean: SieveAd.pr_of_partDYH, SieveAd.solveMDYH_sound, SieveAd.solveD_sound, SieveAd.stepD_sound, SieveAd.rulesMP, SieveAd.resTestsFYH_sound, SieveAd.altFDYH_sound, SieveAd.bd_flipCostDYH, SieveAd.rules_frozResYH, SieveAd.kidsM_sound, SieveAd.kidsF_sound, SieveAd.kidsC_sound, SieveAd.partD_sound, SieveAd.pr_split, SieveAd.pr_mono; at 21/10: Linnik21000.part21000, Linnik21000.soundF21000
Assume Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}, and let $2 < L \le 5/2$ be rational. Whatever the hints and the
search's parameters (the way the configurations of the main tree are read from the hints, the grids of the cells of
the new primes, the depths of recursion of the search on boxes and of the search over frozen parts, and the bound, at
most $11$ (Definition~\ref{def:reversal}), on the number of primes of a reversed box):
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] if the search with the nodes of Definition~\ref{def:tree-b}, on a box of $k \ge 1$ primes with a sign and a
  limit, returns a value $c$ below the limit, then the term of the box with that sign is bounded at $c/\mathcal K$;
\item[(b)] let $\mathfrak b$ be a box of $k \ge 0$ primes, $\varepsilon$ a sign and $\alpha_0 < \alpha_1 < \dots <
  \alpha_N$ integers. If the values of the search on the boxes $\mathfrak b \oplus (\alpha_{i - 1}, \alpha_i)$,
  $1 \le i \le N$, with the sign $\varepsilon$, the hints read in turn and each with the limit $\mathcal K$ less
  the values before it, are each below its limit and add up to at most $c < \mathcal K$, then the term of $\mathfrak b
  \oplus (\alpha_0, \alpha_N)$ with the sign $\varepsilon$ is bounded at $c/\mathcal K$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) is the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:search}, by induction on the depth of recursion, with the cases of
Definition~\ref{def:tree-b}. A value below the limit comes from a node whose condition the search has evaluated to true.
\begin{itemize}
\item An evaluation whose kernel's test of (W) or (W$'$) passes, with or without the cofactor, is a node (E), by
  Lemma~\ref{lem:window-kernel}(c); one whose test (I) passes is a node (E) as before.
\item A step of Buchstab's identity whose test passes is a node (B), its test being (J2) or (J3) with (L) or (L$_3$),
  and Lemma~\ref{lem:tests3}(a) evaluates its terms at the floor; the values of the cells of the new prime add up by
  cuts (S), since any point strictly inside a range is a cut.
\item A reversal of either sign has passed the conditions of Definition~\ref{def:reversal} on the box, with at most the
  search's bound on its number of primes, and the test at its floor, over the wide part for the sign $-$ and over the
  narrow part for the sign $+$ (Section~\ref{sec:reversal-plus}). By the same induction with the nodes over that part
  (Lemma~\ref{lem:window-kernel}(c) for the window's tests, Lemma~\ref{lem:tests3}(c) for the tests at a floor), its
  tree over that part has a value below $64\mathcal K$, which is the cost $c_F$ of a tree that passes. Then
  Proposition~\ref{prop:reversal} or~\ref{prop:reversal-plus}(b) bounds the term at the reversal's cost.
\item A branch whose condition fails returns its limit, which says nothing.
\end{itemize}
(b) follows from (a) by induction on $N$: each value is below its limit, so (a) bounds each cell's
term, and Lemma~\ref{lem:box-identities}(a) adds the bounds.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The strategy and the hints at \texorpdfstring{$21/10$}{21/10}}

The strategy at $21/10$ differs from that of Section~\ref{sec:certificate} in the following; as there, it needs no
proof, and its choices affect only the cost.
\begin{itemize}
\item The grid of a new prime's cells, fixed in the search that the kernel follows (\lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak
  solve21000}, \lean{SieveAd.\allowbreak stepOf}): cells of side at most a tenth of their lower end and at most
  $3298534883/D$, about $0.003$, for the first four primes of a box of the main tree, twice that for the fifth and
  sixth and three times for the seventh and eighth; over a frozen part, $3298534883/D$ for the chain's first two primes
  and twice that from the third.
\item Depth $8$: boxes of fewer than eight primes are decomposed. Reversed: boxes of two to six primes, of either sign
  (Section~\ref{sec:reversal-plus}).
\item Over a frozen part: chains of fewer than three primes are decomposed, as before. The search outside the kernel
  tries every configuration of (I$_{\mathcal M}$) that the hints can name, not the strategy's proposal alone. For a
  step of Buchstab's identity it tries the sixteen floors that the four bits of a hint can name
  (Section~\ref{sec:certificate}), and takes the greatest, at most the lower end of the newest cell, at which the sets
  that the hint names pass.
\item Besides (I), the tests of the resonant window: (W) for a rest of the box's primes, with the cofactor when the
  box's cells leave room for one prime, and (W$'$) where no rest passes (W). Consecutive cells that one test of the
  window evaluates are one cell.
\item Cuts in two: a box is cut in two at the middle of its newest cell, up to three times, where the top of that cell
  passes the window. A box of two primes of the main tree is cut in two at the middle of its newest cell, down to half
  the grid's step, where that lowers its cost with the kernel's time priced as in the next item.
\item The price of the kernel's time: each step is chosen by its cost plus $0.3 \cdot 10^{-6}\mathcal K$ for each
  second of the kernel's time that the search outside the kernel predicts for it, from a table of the kernel's time
  for a step of each kind.
\end{itemize}
% Lean (the search at 21/10; the strategy is tools/linnik/hint_cert.py, CERTS['21000']): SieveAd.solve21000, SieveAd.solveD, SieveAd.stepD, SieveAd.kidsM, SieveAd.kidsF, SieveAd.kidsC, SieveAd.kidsCS, SieveAd.stepOf, SieveAd.cfgIMYH, SieveAd.cfgIFYH, SieveAd.cfgJM, SieveAd.cfgJF, SieveAd.altFDYH, SieveAd.partD

The hints are those of Section~\ref{sec:certificate}, with two changes. The code of an evaluation has one more bit,
which chooses between (I) and the window: \texttt{00} evaluates the box by (I), and \texttt{01} by the window, with
the rest $\mathcal S_r$ and then the records of the test. A record of (W) gives the end of one interval of the cut of
$(0, 1]$ that the hint makes (Section~\ref{sec:window-tests}), and the upper line taken on that interval for the clamp
and for each member of the rest; those of (W$'$) do the same at each of its two corners, and \S S7 gives their layout
in bits. In the main tree the sets of an evaluation have one more bit, the last, for the cofactor, as the sets over a
frozen part already had at $43/20$; (I) does not read it. As at $43/20$, the code \texttt{1110} reverses a box of
either sign, with the floor and the configuration of the test over the wide or the narrow part. The other codes, the
configurations at a floor and the codes of the cuts are those of Section~\ref{sec:certificate}.

\subsection{The certificate at \texorpdfstring{$21/10$}{21/10}}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:certificate-b}
% Lean: Linnik21000.tree21000 (the bound of the first term), Linnik21000.linnik_21000 (the root's test, by evaluation), Linnik21000.part21000, Linnik21000.sound21000, Linnik21000.soundF21000, SieveAd.fillTests_sound, SieveAd.resTestsFYH_sound, SieveAd.pr_of_partDYH, SieveAd.pr_buch, SieveAd.pr_split, SieveAd.pr_mono
Let $L = 21/10$ and $f_* = 33536647203$. The box of no prime passes (J3), with (L) or (L$_3$), at $f_*$ with $\mathcal
S_0 = \mathcal S_1 = \emptyset$ and $\iota = 1$, and the term of the box $((f_*, D/2 + 1))$ with the sign $-$ is bounded
at $c/\mathcal K$, by the search of this section on the parts of the certificate and by the steps of Buchstab's
identity that join them
(Lemmas~\ref{lem:search-b}(b), \ref{lem:tests3}(a) and~\ref{lem:box-identities}), with
\[
  c = 1126600180626425749 = 0.97716989\ldots\, \mathcal K < 0.97717\, \mathcal K.
\]
\end{proposition}

The proposition is a computation, which the kernel carries out and, at the time of writing, has not finished
(Section~\ref{sec:lean}); with Theorem~\ref{thm:tree}, it gives Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}. At the root, $f_*/D =
0.0305014\ldots$, the integer $r_1$ of (J3) (Definition~\ref{def:tests}) is $261788484$, and the admissible range of
(J3) is $[u, v]$ with $u = 523503090992$ and $v = 557039738196$, that is
$[0.4761233, 0.5066247]$ in exponents of $x$: $f_* = v - u - 1$ is the largest floor with $u + f_* < v$, and (L) holds
with $r_2 = r_1 + f_* + 1$, $(u + r_2)/D = 0.5068628\ldots < 1 - 1/L = 0.5238095\ldots$, so the root does not need
(L$_3$). As at $43/20$, the
admissible range is wider than the floor only below about $(L - 2)/((2 - \lambda)L) = 0.0306$.

The check is in parts of the certificate, as at $43/20$. The first prime's range $[f_*, D/2 + 1)$ is cut into $150$
pieces. Three parts, with $11$ cells of side $3298534883/D$, cover $[f_*, 69820530916/D) = [0.0305014, 0.0635014)$.
The other $147$ pieces are cells, $145$ of side $3298534883/D$ from $69820530916/D$ to $548108088951/D =
0.4985014\ldots$ and two shorter ones up to $D/2 + 1$; each has its own step of Buchstab's identity, taken outside the
parts, and the next prime's range cut again into parts and further cells. In all $29349$ cells are cut so: $147$,
$6414$, $22334$ and $454$ of the first, second, third and
fourth primes. $27806$ of their steps of Buchstab's identity use (J3) and $1543$ use (J2), and $24041$ are at the floor
$f_*$. These steps are checked with the tests (J2) and (J3) with (L) or (L$_3$); by a computation outside the
kernel, ten of them, all (J3), eight on boxes of two primes and two on boxes of three, pass with (L$_3$) only. There
are $107374$ parts, in $1150$ files, and the cells' steps are in $58$ files. Lemma~\ref{lem:box-identities}(a) and~(b)
join the pieces, and their costs add up to $c$ exactly. The pieces' costs show where the cost goes (each row ends at
the end of a piece):
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lc@{}}
\toprule
$\log p_1/\log x$ & cost$/\mathcal K$ \\
\midrule
$[0.0305, 0.1025)$ & $0.0005$ \\
$[0.1025, 0.2015)$ & $0.0119$ \\
$[0.2015, 0.3005)$ & $0.1343$ \\
$[0.3005, 0.4025)$ & $0.4597$ \\
$[0.4025, 0.5]$ & $0.3707$ \\
\midrule
all & $0.9772$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The rows and the total are rounded separately. The pieces of the first prime that lie wholly above $x^{0.3}$ cost
$0.8305\mathcal K$, $85\%$ of $c$, and those that lie wholly below $x^{0.2}$ cost $0.0106\mathcal K$. The most expensive
cells cost about $0.015\mathcal K$ each,
near $p_1 = x^{0.40}$ and $x^{0.497}$: $0.01524\mathcal K$ on $[0.3965, 0.3995)$ and $0.01547\mathcal K$ on $[0.4955,
0.4985)$. Figure~\ref{fig:cost} shows how the two costs are spread over the first prime: half of the cost lies above
$x^{0.382}$ at $43/20$ and above $x^{0.379}$ at $21/10$.

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\input{fig-cost}

Propositions~\ref{prop:certificate} and~\ref{prop:certificate-b} is cut into pieces, $104$ at $43/20$ (solid) and
$150$ at $21/10$ (dashed). Over a piece $[a, b)$ of cost $c'$ the curve is at height $(c'/\mathcal K)/((b - a)/D)$,
the cost per unit of $\log p_1/\log x$, so the area under each curve is $C = c/\mathcal K$: $0.94846\ldots$ at $43/20$
and $0.97716\ldots$ at $21/10$. The three parts of the certificate at the left end of each curve are each drawn as one
step, at their mean height, since their cost is not split by cell. The gray

The plan has $90137757$ steps, and its hints take $501724020$ bytes. The search outside the kernel that made it visits
$5141981623$ boxes. It planned the first prime's cells in about three to four hours on four cores of each of two
machines. It predicts $273632$ seconds of one core of the kernel's time for the $1150$ files of the parts of the
certificate, about $76$ hours; this is a prediction, not a measurement. \S S5 has the other counts.

\subsection{Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}]
Take $L = 21/10$. As in Section~\ref{sec:proof}, Theorem~\ref{thm:type2}, under Input~\ref{inp:half-plane}, gives the
comparisons of Section~\ref{sec:type1} at $L$ and with them the tests of Section~\ref{sec:decomp}.
Theorem~\ref{thm:type2-leaves}, under Inputs~\ref{inp:half-plane} and~\ref{inp:cgl}, gives the estimate in two variables
on the leaves' region $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$, and with it Propositions~\ref{prop:harman-two3}
and~\ref{prop:harman-fill3} and the tests with (L) or (L$_3$), Lemma~\ref{lem:tests3}. Theorem~\ref{thm:window}, under
the same two inputs, gives the window, and with it the tests (W) and (W$'$), Lemmas~\ref{lem:window-tests}
and~\ref{lem:window-kernel}. Propositions~\ref{prop:reversal} and~\ref{prop:reversal-plus} give the reversals of
either sign. So Proposition~\ref{prop:tree-bound} holds for the trees of Definition~\ref{def:tree-b},
Lemma~\ref{lem:search-b} for their search, and Theorem~\ref{thm:tree} with the root's test (J3) taken with (L) or
(L$_3$). Proposition~\ref{prop:certificate-b} gives a floor $f_*$ at which the box of no prime passes (J3) with (L) or
(L$_3$) (in fact with (L), as noted after the proposition), and bounds the term of the root box with the sign $-$ at
$c/\mathcal K$, with $c = 1126600180626425749 < \mathcal K = 2^{60}$. Theorem~\ref{thm:tree} gives
Theorem~\ref{thm:2110} under the two inputs.

Input~\ref{inp:half-plane} enters as in Section~\ref{sec:proof}, and through the bins of the zeros in the window of
Theorem~\ref{thm:window} and in the estimate on the leaves' region. Input~\ref{inp:cgl} enters in two places: through
the count of the large values of the powers of a prime sum, Lemma~\ref{lem:lv-powers}, in the window; and through the
curve $g^*$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:curve-star}, in the estimate on the leaves' region. Ingham's and Huxley's density
estimates enter through $g^*$ and $g_{\mathrm H}$.
\end{proof}

The remarks after the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:4320} hold here as well: the proof does not compute the constant $K$
of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}, no constant
is claimed to be effective, and the thresholds are enormous.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:count} at $21/10$]
Take $L = 21/10$ and
\[
  C = \frac{1126600180626425749}{\mathcal K},
\]
the cost of Proposition~\ref{prop:certificate-b} over $\mathcal K$. As in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110},
Theorem~\ref{thm:tree} applies with this $C$ under the two
inputs, and its count (b) is part (b) of the corollary. Lemma~\ref{lem:every-x}, with $c_0 = (1 - C)/2$, gives
part (a) of the corollary, with $c = (1 - C)/2^{L + 2}$, as at $43/20$.
\end{proof}

\section{The formalisation}\label{sec:lean}

This section says which Lean theorems state the paper's results and with which axioms they are checked, what Lean's
kernel has not yet checked, what is not formalised, and what is known of the independent checking of the inputs.
Appendix~\ref{app:claims} names the Lean theorems that prove each numbered result of Sections~\ref{sec:intro}
to~\ref{sec:certificate-b}, and the results from the literature that the proofs use; they are in \file{lib/} of the
repository \cite{repo} or in Mathlib. \file{formalisations/} documents the results from the literature and the further
classical results behind them. The Lean statements of Theorems~\ref{thm:2110} and~\ref{thm:4320} are
{\small
\begin{verbatim}
theorem linnik_21000 : ∃ K : ℝ, ∀ (q : ℕ) [NeZero q] (a : ℕ),
    a.Coprime q → ∃ p : ℕ, p.Prime ∧ p ≡ a [MOD q] ∧
      (p : ℝ) ≤ K * (q : ℝ) ^ (2100 / 1000 : ℝ)

theorem linnik_21500 : ∃ K : ℝ, ∀ (q : ℕ) [NeZero q] (a : ℕ),
    a.Coprime q → ∃ p : ℕ, p.Prime ∧ p ≡ a [MOD q] ∧
      (p : ℝ) ≤ K * (q : ℝ) ^ (2150 / 1000 : ℝ)
\end{verbatim}
}
where \lean{[NeZero q]} says $q \ge 1$, the power is the real power, $2100/1000 = 21/10$ and $2150/1000 = 43/20$. The
two theorems are in the namespaces \lean{Linnik21000} and \lean{Linnik21500}, whose names carry the exponent as
$2.1000$ and $2.1500$.
Corollary~\ref{cor:count} is \lean{Linnik21000.\allowbreak count\_21000} and
\lean{Linnik21000.\allowbreak count\_large\_21000} at $21/10$, in \file{lib/pnt/Linnik21000Count.lean}, and
\lean{Linnik21500.\allowbreak count\_21500} and \lean{Linnik21500.\allowbreak count\_large\_21500} at $43/20$, in
\file{lib/pnt/Linnik21500Count.lean}, with no hypotheses either; there $\pi(x; q, a)$ counts the primes $p \le
\lfloor x \rfloor$, and the interval $(q^L, 2q^L]$ is $(\lfloor q^L \rfloor, \lfloor 2q^L \rfloor]$, which has the same
integers. At $21/10$ the corollary uses the certificate of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110} and has the same status (see below).

The file \file{lib/check/Check.lean}, the
repository's default build, prints the axioms of Theorem~\ref{thm:4320}, of Corollary~\ref{cor:count} at $43/20$ and
of the library's theorems that Appendix~\ref{app:claims} names for Sections~\ref{sec:inputs} to~\ref{sec:proof}, for
the results from the literature and for the classical deduction;
\file{lib/check/CheckAll.lean} prints those of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}, of Corollary~\ref{cor:count} at $21/10$ and of
the theorems named for Sections~\ref{sec:window} to~\ref{sec:certificate-b}. \file{scripts/check\_axioms.py} checks
that they are \lean{propext}, \lean{Classical.choice} and \lean{Quot.sound} only. \file{lib/check/CheckAll.lean}
imports the certificate at $21/10$, so its report waits for the build described below. The axioms of a theorem cover its
whole dependency
cone, so a \lean{sorry} anywhere in it would appear as \lean{sorryAx}; the library's own sources contain no
\lean{sorry}, no declared axiom and no \lean{native\_decide} (\file{scripts/check\_hygiene.py}).

The certificates of Propositions~\ref{prop:certificate} and~\ref{prop:certificate-b} are checked by evaluation in
Lean's kernel, which trusts no code compiled from the definitions (Section~\ref{sec:certificate}); the definitions are
arithmetic on Lean's core natural numbers, lists and booleans. At $43/20$ the search led by the certificate's hints is
evaluated in $1552$ parts of the certificate, in $17$ files that the kernel checks in $143$ to $271$ seconds each,
about $63$ minutes in all, and the $256$ Buchstab steps that join the parts are proved one by one. At $21/10$ it is
evaluated in $107374$ parts, in $1150$ files, and $29349$ Buchstab steps join the parts. The program that wrote the
hints predicts about $76$ hours of the kernel's time on one core for the part files, so the default build includes
neither them nor the $58$ files of the cells' steps; the repository's continuous integration builds them in stages. At
the time of writing that build has not finished, so the kernel has not yet checked the certificate at $21/10$ as a
whole (\S S5); Theorem~\ref{thm:2110} is a theorem of the library once the build at the commit of this paper ends
without error.

The two theorems share Input~\ref{inp:half-plane}, the curve $g_{\mathrm H}$ and Sections~\ref{sec:type2}
to~\ref{sec:decomp}, and differ in their tests. The tests of
Theorem~\ref{thm:4320} are those of Definition~\ref{def:tests}, with the leaves' condition $(\mathrm L)$ alone
(the namespace \lean{SieveAd.Plain}); those of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110} accept the leaves cut in three as well
(Section~\ref{sec:leaves-three}), and their soundness uses the second density curve $g^*$. The proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:4320} uses neither Input~\ref{inp:cgl} nor Ingham's density estimate, and two scripts check this on
the sources. Its import cone contains no module of the library's formalisation of Chen, Gupta and Li's paper
(\file{scripts/import\_cone.py}). It contains modules of Ingham's estimate, but no declaration of that estimate is
reachable from the theorem (\file{scripts/decl\_reach.py}, a static reachability over the identifiers of each proof,
which over-approximates the constants used when, as here, the modules concerned declare no simp lemmas, instances or
attributes); the proof of Huxley's estimate uses Ingham's zero detector, not his density estimate. The
toolchain is Lean \texttt{v4.35.0-rc3} with Mathlib at commit \texttt{0f64d30a}, and the repository's continuous
integration builds the library and checks the axioms on every change to it.

Among the statements that are not Lean theorems are:
\begin{itemize}
\item the historical statements of Sections~\ref{sec:intro} and~\ref{sec:comparison}, among them the remarks on
  Naslund's, Harman's, R.~Li's and Banks and Shparlinski's results, and the exponents that Harman's and R.~Li's
  theorems give with Input~\ref{inp:half-plane};
\item the heuristic remarks of Sections~\ref{sec:intro}, \ref{sec:type2}, \ref{sec:type1}, \ref{sec:window}
  and~\ref{sec:rules}, among them what the region needs of the kernel bound and of a density estimate and why the
  leaves' region needs $g^*$, and the effectivity remark of Section~\ref{sec:proof};
\item the remarks that Chen, Gupta and Li's Theorem~1.2 and Corollary~1.4 give $7/3 + \varepsilon$;
\item the numbers computed by the repository's scripts (the shape of the region at $43/20$ and of the leaves' region
  at $21/10$, the statistics of the searches and of the certificates, and the computations of
  Section~\ref{sec:comparison}), and what Figures~\ref{fig:density}, \ref{fig:region} and~\ref{fig:cost} show;
\item what is said about the sources of the inputs and their independent checking.
\end{itemize}

Independent checking of the inputs is limited. OpenAI's own Lean formalisation of the main preprint's result
\cite{openai-lean} is OpenAI's record, not an independent one, and this library's proof of Input~\ref{inp:half-plane}
is the author's new formalisation, written with the AI assistance of the acknowledgements, of the companion preprint's
route. Chen, Gupta and Li's preprint \cite{cgl} has not been refereed; the library's proof of Input~\ref{inp:cgl}
follows their route (Section~\ref{sec:inputs}). Ingham's and Huxley's estimates are refereed and classical; the
library's proofs of them, by the large values of Dirichlet polynomials and the fourth moment, are the author's,
written with the same assistance. \S S5 gives the details: the Lean statements, the library's proofs of the inputs,
what Lean's kernel checks, and the artefact.

\section{What the inputs give by other means, and the method's limits}\label{sec:comparison}

This section claims nothing new. It places Theorems~\ref{thm:2110} and~\ref{thm:4320}, and
Corollary~\ref{cor:count}(a), against what the same inputs give by the classical argument, which has no sieve, and
against Harman's theorems on primes in a progression and the exponents known for special moduli.
Table~\ref{tab:exponents} sums up the comparisons with the classical argument and with Harman's theorems.

\subsection*{The classical deduction}

Suppose that for some $A \ge 2$ and $B, C \ge 0$ there is a density estimate, uniform in $q$ and $T$,
\[
  N^*(\sigma, T; q) \le C (qT)^{A(1 - \sigma)} (\log qT)^B \qquad (1/2 \le \sigma \le 1,\ T \ge 2).
\]
With Input~\ref{inp:half-plane} it gives Linnik's theorem with every exponent $L > A$. The library's proof writes the
smoothed sum $\sum_{n \equiv a} \Lambda(n) e^{-n/x}$ through the characters, bounds each non-trivial character's sum by
a contour shift to the edge of a rectangle free of its zeros, and counts the characters by the density estimate: those
with zeros of real part about $\sigma$ contribute about $(q^A/x)^{1 - \sigma}$ together, and none has a zero of real
part above $11/12$. With this deduction the library proves:
\begin{itemize}
\item $L = 12 + \varepsilon$ from Input~\ref{inp:half-plane} alone: at most $\varphi(q) \le qT$ characters are
  counted for every $\sigma$, and above $11/12$ at most the trivial character, which Input~\ref{inp:half-plane}
  excepts at $s = 1$; this is the estimate with $A = 12$;
\item $L = 36/13 + \varepsilon$ with Ingham's estimate, whose exponent $3/(2 - \sigma)$ is $36/13$ at
  $\sigma = 11/12$;
\item $L = 12/5 + \varepsilon$ with Huxley's estimate (with Ingham's below $\sigma = 3/4$), which bounds the density
  exponent by $12/5$ on the whole range;
\item $L = 11667/5000 + \varepsilon = 2.3334 + \varepsilon$ with the curve $g^*$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:curve-star},
  whose pieces between $5/7$ and $131/168$ come from Input~\ref{inp:cgl}: its exponent is at most $11667/5000$ on the
  whole range.
\end{itemize}
Appendix~\ref{app:claims} names the Lean theorem of each.

With Chen, Gupta and Li's density estimate the exponent is
$7/3 + \varepsilon$, by the same deduction from their Theorem~1.2 \cite{cgl} (exponent $A = 7/3$), which the library
does not formalise as they state it; the last item is what the library proves in its place, and it differs from $7/3$
by the rounding up of the steps of $g^*$. They do not state a Linnik exponent, but their Corollary~1.4 \cite{cgl}, on
primes in progressions in short intervals under a zero-free region, contains the exponent $7/3 + \varepsilon$. Let
$\psi(x; q, a)$ be the sum of $\Lambda(n)$ over $n \le x$, $n \equiv a \bmod q$. For $h \le x$ their corollary gives
$\psi(x + h; q, a) - \psi(x; q, a) \sim h/\varphi(q)$ whenever
$h/\varphi(q) \ge x^{1/2 + \varepsilon} q^{1/6} + x^{17/30 + \varepsilon}$, and with $h = x$ and
Input~\ref{inp:half-plane} as the zero-free region this holds at $x = q^L$ for every $L > 7/3$ and all large $q$.

\subsection*{Harman's range and special moduli}

Harman \cite{harman05, harman08} applied his sieve to primes in a progression to one large modulus $q$, for Alford,
Granville and Pomerance's construction of Carmichael numbers \cite{agp}, which has the ranges $q \le x^B$ for
$B < 5/12$ outside exceptional moduli. In Harman's construction the exceptional moduli are those for which the
first display below, the condition on the zeros, fails. His Theorem~2 in \cite{harman05} is the following. For every
$\varepsilon > 0$ there are $K(\varepsilon)$ and $c > 0$ with this property. Let $q > K(\varepsilon)$, and suppose
that for every $d \mid q$ and every primitive character $\chi$ mod $d$
\[
  L(s, \chi) \ne 0 \quad \text{for} \quad \operatorname{Re} s > 1 - (\log q)^{-3/4}, \quad
  |\operatorname{Im} s| \le \exp\bigl(\varepsilon (\log q)^{3/4}\bigr).
\]
Then for every $a$ prime to $q$
\[
  \pi(x; q, a) \ge c\, \frac{x}{\varphi(q) \log x} \qquad \text{whenever } q < x^{0.472}.
\]
Harman sketches the proof. Its arithmetical information is the zero-free region, a mean value estimate, the fourth
moment of the $L$-functions and Huxley's large values estimate. He shows that this information is analogous to that
of Harman, Kumchev and Lewis's work on prime ideals in small regions \cite{hkl}, which uses no analogue of Watt's
mean value theorem, and so obtains the range $q < x^{0.47}$; the more careful numerical analysis of Lewis's thesis
\cite{lewis-thesis} gives $q < x^{0.472}$ \cite[p.~646]{harman05}. The comparisons below hold with $0.47$ as well,
since $1/0.47 < 2.128$.

His Theorem~1.2 in \cite{harman08} is this: there is $\delta > 0$ such that the statement above holds, with $0.4736$
in the place of $0.472$, for the moduli $q > K(\varepsilon)$ all of whose prime factors are below $q^\delta$. Its
proof brings in Watt's mean value theorem on sums of character sums, and the condition on the prime factors serves
that theorem's application; Harman proves one lemma in full and sketches the remainder.

Input~\ref{inp:half-plane} implies the condition on the zeros, for every $\varepsilon$, as soon as
$(\log q)^{3/4} \ge 12$. So with Input~\ref{inp:half-plane}, Harman's Theorem~2 applies to every large modulus: the
least prime in every class prime to $q$ is at most $q^{1/0.472}$, and $1/0.472 < 2.1187$. This is Linnik's theorem
for every modulus: the finitely many moduli that are not large are covered by the constant of Linnik's theorem. In
the same way his Theorem~1.2 gives the exponent $1/0.4736 < 2.1115$ for the large moduli all of whose prime factors
are below $q^\delta$. Harman's theorems are in refereed journals, and they need the condition on the zeros for the
divisors of the one modulus only. Their consequences for the least prime use Input~\ref{inp:half-plane} for that
condition, and so rest on a result that has not been refereed, as Theorems~\ref{thm:2110} and~\ref{thm:4320} do.

The exponent of Theorem~\ref{thm:4320} is larger than $1/0.472$, so it is not the smallest that
Input~\ref{inp:half-plane} gives. The theorem is stated because its proof, from Input~\ref{inp:half-plane} and
Huxley's density estimate, is given in full and formalised, and because the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110} builds
on it (Sections~\ref{sec:window} to~\ref{sec:certificate-b} add to Sections~\ref{sec:inputs} to~\ref{sec:proof}).
The exponent of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110} is smaller than $1/0.472$ by $0.0186\ldots$, and its proof uses
Input~\ref{inp:cgl} as well. We have not examined what Harman's argument would give with the inputs of this paper
in the place of its own: the half-plane for its zero-free region, and Input~\ref{inp:cgl} for Huxley's large values
estimate.

For the powers of a fixed prime, exponents smaller than that of Theorem~\ref{thm:4320} have been obtained without
Input~\ref{inp:half-plane}. Banks and Shparlinski \cite{banks-shparlinski} have $2.1115$ (the value as cited in
\cite[footnote~1]{cgl}), from Harman's Theorem~1.2 and their zero-free region (as described by Baier and Pujahari
\cite{baier-pujahari}). R.~Li's Theorem~9 \cite{li-harman} has the range $q < x^{0.4752}$ under the two conditions
of Harman's Theorem~1.2, with a constant $c_1 > 0$ in the place of $\delta$. For the powers of a fixed prime he
deduces the exponent $1/0.4752 < 2.1044$ (his Theorem~11),
improving Banks and Shparlinski's exponent. He presents these theorems as modifications of his Harman-sieve
calculation, without separate proofs. If his Theorem~9 holds as stated, it gives with Input~\ref{inp:half-plane} the
exponent $1/0.4752 = 2.1043\ldots$ for every large modulus whose prime factors are all below $q^{c_1}$. Every
exponent of this subsection lies between those of Theorems~\ref{thm:2110} and~\ref{thm:4320}.

Corollary~\ref{cor:count}(a) gives the lower bound of Harman's theorem, $\pi(x; q, a) \gg x/(\varphi(q) \log x)$,
for every modulus $q \le (x/K)^{10/21}$, $10/21 = 0.4761\ldots$, and, from Input~\ref{inp:half-plane} and Huxley's
estimate alone, for every $q \le (x/K)^{20/43}$, $20/43 = 0.4651\ldots$, with $K$ depending on the exponent. The
ranges $0.472$, $0.4736$ and $0.4752$ above lie between these two: the corollary's range is shorter than each of
them at $20/43$, and longer at $10/21$. The corollary uses Input~\ref{inp:half-plane}, and at $10/21$
Input~\ref{inp:cgl} as well, which have not been refereed; Harman's theorems assume their condition on the zeros
for the one modulus.

\subsection*{What the half-plane is used for}

The classical deduction does not need the value $11/12$ of Input~\ref{inp:half-plane}: it works with any zero-free
half-plane $\operatorname{Re} s > 1 - \eta_0$, for a fixed $\eta_0 > 0$. The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:4320} uses the
half-plane in the places that the end of its proof lists (Section~\ref{sec:proof}): for the prime sums of
Lemma~\ref{lem:prime-sum}, whose contour runs to its edge, for the top bin of Section~\ref{sec:bins}, for the kernel
bound, where the Pólya--Vinogradov inequality would do (Section~\ref{sec:region}), for the piece of $g_{\mathrm H}$
above $11/12$, and for the prime number theorem. With a narrower half-plane the bins would run up to $1 -
\eta_0$, and the zone condition for two variables of length $q$ at the top bin would need the exponent $\rho$ of the
rest at least about
$1/(25000\eta_0)$, where $\rho \ge 1/2000$ suffices at $\eta_0 = 1/12$; the region, and so the certificate, would
change, and this is not pursued. The proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:2110} uses the half-plane in the same places, and in the bins of its two further estimates. In the
estimate in one variable the half-plane bounds every prime sum of the rest of length $Q$ by about $Q^{11/12}$, through
Lemma~\ref{lem:prime-sum}, and the classes of characters that no count makes small are bounded by that alone
(Section~\ref{sec:window-estimate}). What the proofs use is that there are no zeros near $\operatorname{Re} s = 1$, and
in particular no exceptional zero.

\subsection*{Where the gain comes from, and its limits}

The deduction needs the zeros of every real part to contribute less than the main term, so its exponent is the
largest density exponent on $[1/2, 11/12]$, which is $12/5$ with Huxley's estimate, at $\sigma = 3/4$, and $7/3$ with
Chen, Gupta and Li's, at $\sigma = 5/7$. The sieve of Theorem~\ref{thm:4320} meets the zeros only in sums over
$n = n_1 n_2 r$, with $n_1$ and $n_2$ of lengths near $q$ and $r$ a product of primes, where Theorem~\ref{thm:type2}
holds for every $L$ and uses the
density estimate only above $0.7789$, and it recovers the primes from those sums at the cost that its certificate
bounds. In exponents of $x$ the region shrinks as $L$ grows smaller, so more of the sieve's terms fall outside it.

With the rules of Sections~\ref{sec:type2} to~\ref{sec:certificate} alone the cost is $0.94846\ldots$ at $43/20$. On
a sample of twenty of the first prime's cells, taking each test at the midpoint of its cell, which gives no
certificate, lowers the cost by about a quarter. Each $0.005$ of exponent costs between $5\%$ and $9\%$ of the cost,
as measured with a coarser search. The rules that this certificate lacks, the reversal of the terms of the sign $+$
among them, are worth a few per cent each, measured in exact integers on the same twenty cells (\S S6).

The rules of Sections~\ref{sec:window} and~\ref{sec:rules} are of another kind. The estimate in one variable evaluates
terms that are outside the region of two variables. Where each prime sum of a term's rest has a power of length near
$q$, the mean value of that power counts the characters at which the sum is large as well as a density exponent $2$
would (Section~\ref{sec:intro}). The leaves cut in three recover comparisons that the
bilinear
Type~I estimate does
not reach. With them, the reversal of the terms of the sign $+$, the test at the two corners and cells of side $0.003$,
the search passes at $21/10$ with the cost $0.97716\ldots$ when it tries every configuration over the frozen parts.
Its plan has $90137757$ steps against $3400745$ at $43/20$ (Sections~\ref{sec:certificate}
and~\ref{sec:certificate-b}), about twenty-six and a half times as many. So about
$0.023$ of the primes is left at
$21/10$, of which Corollary~\ref{cor:count}(b) keeps half. With so little left, $21/10$ is close to what these rules
give as the search uses them. Going much further needs
Type~I information on longer products, or Type~II estimates on wider regions, which a better density estimate above
$0.7789$ would give only a little of (Section~\ref{sec:region}).

this paper. The author is responsible for the paper's content.

Grok Bot, an AI research assistant built by xAI, also assisted: with the literature and the analysis of the density
bounds, with the certificate pipeline, with edits to this paper, and with a survey and refactoring of the Lean library
for duplication and build cost. The Lean verification is by Lean's kernel and the repository's continuous
integration, and not by the assistants.

\appendix

\section{The claims table}\label{app:claims}

The table names the Lean theorems behind each result of the paper, behind the results from the literature that its
argument uses directly, and behind the main results from the literature on which the two inputs and the density
estimates rest. It has four blocks: Theorem~\ref{thm:4320}, with Corollary~\ref{cor:count} at $43/20$ and
Sections~\ref{sec:inputs} to~\ref{sec:proof}; Theorem~\ref{thm:2110}, with Corollary~\ref{cor:count} at $21/10$ and
Sections~\ref{sec:window} to~\ref{sec:certificate-b}; the literature; and the classical deduction. An indented row
belongs to the last unindented row above it: a further part of its statement, a step of its proof, or a result it
rests on. The names against a claim state or prove it, and a mark (\S S$n$) points to the section of the supplement
that proves the step.

The file \file{lib/check/Check.lean} prints the axioms of the theorems of the first, third and fourth blocks, and
\file{lib/check/CheckAll.lean} those of the second; Section~\ref{sec:lean} says how they are checked. Where the table
elides steps with dots, \file{lib/check/Check.lean} prints the first and the last, and a theorem that uses every step,
so all of them are in a dependency cone whose axioms are checked. For Huxley's fifteen steps on $[131/168, 5/6)$ that
theorem is \lean{SieveCurveHx.counts\_hx}, in the cone of \lean{Linnik21500.linnik\_21500}; for the steps of $g^*$
(those of Input~\ref{inp:cgl} from $0$ to $29$, and Ingham's from $0$ to $18$) it is \lean{SieveCurve7D.counts\_7d}.
Dirichlet's theorem, \lean{Nat.forall\_exists\_prime\_gt\_and\_modEq}, is Mathlib's, and neither file prints it; it is
in the dependency cone of \lean{Linnik21500.linnik\_21500}, whose axioms are checked, and in that of
\lean{Linnik21000.linnik\_21000}.

A name's first component is its namespace, which is usually also the name of its file in \file{lib/pnt}; a list
after the table gives the exceptions.

\section{Terms and notation}\label{app:terms}

This appendix lists the paper's own terms, in the order in which the paper introduces them, and the symbols that
occur in more than one section. Each row recalls a definition in one line and names the place where it is stated in
full; a letter with a second meaning in another section has a row for each meaning.

\subsection*{Terms}

{\footnotesize
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.19\textwidth}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.61\textwidth}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.15\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Term & Meaning & Where \\
\midrule
\endhead
density curve & $g$ with, for some $C \ge 0$, $N^*(\sigma, T; q) \le C(qT)^{g(\sigma)}(1 + \log qT)^{10}$ for every
  $q \ge 1$, $1/2 \le \sigma \le 1$, $T \ge 2$ & Def.~\ref{def:curve} \\
first, second variable & $n_1$, $n_2$ in $n = n_1 n_2 r$, of lengths $P_1 = q^{m_1}$, $P_2 = q^{m_2}$ &
  \S\ref{sec:type2} \\
rest & the primes that supply the prime sums, and their product; $\rho$ is its exponent & \S\ref{sec:type2} \\
region $\mathcal R_L$ & the $(m_1, m_2)$ with $\rho \ge 1/2000$, $m_i \ge 2/3$, and $s_1, s_2, s_1 + s_2 \le
  \lambda\rho - \mu$ &
  Def.~\ref{def:region} \\
bin & of $\chi$: the largest $i < I$ with a zero in $\operatorname{Re} s > \beta_i$, $|\operatorname{Im} s| \le T$, or
  $0$ & \S\ref{sec:bins} \\
kernel $k_Y$; kernel bound & $\sum_{n \ge 1} \psi(n) e^{-n/Y}$; $|k_Y(\psi)| \le C Y^{1/12 - \delta'} (2q)^{5/12 +
  \delta' + 2\varepsilon'}$, $\psi$ non-trivial & \S\ref{sec:two-bounds} \\
zone condition & of a set $\mathcal R$, for $g$ at $L$: at all $1/2 \le \beta \le 11/12$, $0 \le \varpi \le 4 \cdot
  10^{-6}$, $(m_1, m_2) \in \mathcal R$, some exponents $M_i$ of the mean squares (\S\ref{sec:zone}) give $E \le L -
  1/25000$ & Def.~\ref{def:zone} \\
comparison; pivot & a condition $c + \sum_i e_i \log y_i > 0$ on primes, $e_i \in \{-1, 0, 1\}$; an $i$ with $e_i =
  \pm 1$ & \S\ref{sec:type1} \\
family & in two variables: two polynomials' data, prime sums and comparisons; of a box: its tuples $\mathcal
  F(\mathfrak b)$ & \S\S\ref{sec:family-two}, \ref{sec:boxes} \\
Harman's comparison & Buchstab's identity from $z_0$ down to $w$ (the comparison's tree); Lemma~\ref{lem:term-two}
  evaluates the stops &
  Prop.~\ref{prop:harman-two} \\
stop; leaf & the sum $S(\mathcal A_W, p)$ of a branch of the comparison's tree stopped before $w$; the sum
  $S(\mathcal A_d, w)$ of one that reaches $w$ & \S\ref{sec:harman} \\
cell; box & $(l, h)$, for the primes of $[x^{l/D}, x^{h/D})$; a list $\mathfrak b$ of cells, one for each prime (in
  \S\ref{sec:corners}, also a product of intervals of exponents) & \S\ref{sec:boxes} \\
floor & the sifting floor $f$, for the sifting level $z_f = \lceil x^{f/D} \rceil$ (not $\lfloor \cdot \rfloor$) &
  \S\ref{sec:boxes} \\
term (at a floor) & $T(\mathcal C, \mathfrak b) = \sum_{\mathcal F(\mathfrak b)} S(\mathcal C_r, p_k)$; at $f$, with
  $z_f$ for $p_k$ & \S\ref{sec:boxes} \\
evaluated; bounded at $C$ & $\Delta(\mathfrak b) = o(X)$; $\varepsilon\Delta(\mathfrak b) \ge -(C + \eta)X$ for every
  $\eta > 0$, $q$ large & \S\ref{sec:boxes} \\
tree; passes; cost & nodes (V)\ldots(R) with the trees below; all conditions hold; the drops' and reversals' costs &
  Def.~\ref{def:tree} \\
test & a condition on a box's exponents under which its terms are evaluated: (I), (J2), (J3); (W), (W$'$) &
  Def.~\ref{def:tests}, \ref{def:window-tests} \\
admissible range & $\mathcal W(\rho_-, \rho_+) = [u, v]$: the first variables (units of $1/D$) that the region allows
  with every rest near $[\rho_-, \rho_+]$ &
  \S\ref{sec:tests} \\
leaves' condition; growth & (L): inequalities on the leaves' exponents $a_1$, $a_2$ under which
  Lemma~\ref{lem:leaves} applies; $g$ bounds
  their new primes & Def.~\ref{def:tests} \\
drop; share & a term of the sign $+$ bounded through $T(\mathcal A, \mathfrak b) \ge 0$; $\sum_{\mathcal F(\mathfrak
  b)} \Phi(x/r,
  p_k)$, which bounds the loss & \S\ref{sec:dropped} \\
reversal & a term counted as $n = p_1 R$, over $R$; for a box of the sign $-$, a floor and a tree over the wide part;
  with the sign $+$, over the narrow part & Def.~\ref{def:reversal}, \ref{def:reversal-plus} \\
frozen part; wide, narrow; ends & the pairs $(\mathbf a, m)$ of a tuple $\mathbf a$ of the other primes and a cofactor
  $m$ with $P^-(m) \ge a_k$, $R = a_2 \cdots a_k m \in (x^{e_-/D}, x^{e_+/D}]$; the ends: wide less narrow &
  \S\ref{sec:reversal} \\
level of the reversal & $\nu = \lceil h_1/2 \rceil$, the upper end of the cell of the reversal's chain $((f, \nu))$ &
  \S\ref{sec:reversal} \\
chain & a box $\mathfrak c$ of primes over a frozen part, in $n/R$ & \S\ref{sec:reversal} \\
tree over the frozen part & a tree on a chain, nodes (V$_{\mathcal M}$), (E$_{\mathcal M}$), (D$_{\mathcal M}$), (S),
  (B) & Def.~\ref{def:frozen-tree} \\
search; limit & from a box, a sign and a limit, an integer: below the limit, a cost that bounds the term &
  \S\ref{sec:search} \\
strategy; depth & what the search tries, needing no proof; the number of primes below which a box is decomposed &
  \S\ref{sec:search} \\
configuration & the sets and the side that a test takes & \S\ref{sec:search} \\
Lean's kernel & Lean's checker; it decides propositions about natural numbers, lists and Booleans by
  evaluation & \S\ref{sec:checking} \\
certificate; part of the certificate & the hints and the propositions that Lean's kernel checks; a range of cells
  checked as one unit & \S\S\ref{sec:checking}, \ref{sec:certificate} \\
plan; hints & the choices that the search outside Lean's kernel kept; the plan written down for Lean's kernel &
  \S\S\ref{sec:certificate}, \ref{sec:hints} \\
the window & the resonant window, the Type~II estimate in one variable, and its tests (W), (W$'$) &
  \S\ref{sec:window} \\
variable & in Section~\ref{sec:window} and the tests (W), (W$'$): $W$, of length $q^m$; in a term, $n$ outside the
  rest & \S\ref{sec:window-estimate} \\
level of a prime sum; class & $\ell_j(\chi) \approx \tau^{-1} \log_{Q_j} |S_{Q_j}(\chi, h_j)|$; a bin refined by the
  levels & \S\ref{sec:window-estimate} \\
size of a prime sum; count exponent & $t_j$, with $|S_{Q_j}| \le 2Q_j^{t_j}$ on a class; $\gamma$, with about
  $q^\gamma$ characters in it & \S\ref{sec:window-estimate} \\
admissible tuple & $(\beta, \gamma, t_1, \dots, t_K)$ with $\gamma \le g_{\mathrm H}(\beta)$, $t_j \le \beta + 4
  \cdot 10^{-6}$, and $\gamma$ below the counts at $t_j - \tau$ & Def.~\ref{def:res} \\
fit & of $(E, \gamma, m)$: orthogonality's bound, or both Hal\'asz--Montgomery bounds, at most $L - 1/25000$ &
  Def.~\ref{def:fit} \\
resonance condition & every admissible tuple $(\beta, \gamma, t_1, \dots, t_K)$ gives exponents that fit &
  Def.~\ref{def:res} \\
main tree; end node & the tree on the boxes, not over a frozen part; a node with no tree below it: (V), (E), (D), (P)
  & \S\S\ref{sec:window-terms}, \ref{sec:certificate-b} \\
leaves' region & $\mathcal R^{\mathrm{lf}}_L$, for a leaf's $d_1$ (first variable), cofactor (second variable) and
  $d_2$ (rest) &
  Def.~\ref{def:leaf-region} \\
size of a leaf & the number of primes that the comparison's tree adds to the tuple & \S\ref{sec:leaves-three} \\
leaves cut in three & cuts $d_1 \le q^{D_1^*}$, then $d_2 \le q^{D_2^*}$, and the third range; tested by
  (L$_3$) & \S\ref{sec:leaves-three} \\
tree of Theorem~\ref{thm:2110} & with (W), (W$'$) in (E), (L$_3$) in (B), and the node (R$^+$) &
  Def.~\ref{def:tree-b} \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
}

\subsection*{Notation}

Exponents of $q$: $m_i$, $\rho$, $s_i$, $s_j$, $D_i$, $D_i^*$, and $l_i$, $h_i$, $t$, $u$, $v$ in
Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two}. Exponents of $x = q^L$: $a_1$, $a_2$. Units of $1/D$ of exponents of $x$ (the
integer $e$ for $x^{e/D} = q^{Le/D}$): cells, floors, $\rho_\pm$, the admissible range $[u, v]$, $r_1$, $r_2$, the
growth $g$, the $A$, $B$ of (L$_3$), the room $\gamma$, the ends $e_\pm$ of a frozen part. The sizes $t_j$ are
exponents of $Q_j$. Costs are integers in units of $1/\mathcal K$.

Symbol & Meaning & Where \\

\multicolumn{3}{@{}l}{\emph{The problem and the sets}} \\
$q$, $a$; $\chi_0$; $\mathcal X^*$ & the modulus, a residue prime to it; the trivial character; the others &

$x$ & any real $x \ge Kq^L$ & Lem.~\ref{lem:every-x} \\
$K$ & the constant of Theorems~\ref{thm:2110} and~\ref{thm:4320} & Thm.~\ref{thm:2110} \\
$K$ & the number of prime sums & Thm.~\ref{thm:type2} \\
$N^*(\sigma, T; q)$ & the number of $\chi \bmod q$ with a zero in $\operatorname{Re} s \ge \sigma$,
  $|\operatorname{Im} s| \le T$ & \S\ref{sec:inputs} \\

  \rfloor$, $\lfloor 2x \rfloor$ & \S\ref{sec:type1} \\
$S(\mathcal C_r, z)$; $\vartheta$ & the number of $n \in \mathcal C$, $r \mid n$, with no prime factor below $z$;
  $\mathbf 1[n \equiv a] - \mathbf 1[(n, q) = 1]/\varphi(q)$ & \S\ref{sec:type1} \\
$X$; $o(X)$ & $x/(\varphi(q) \log x)$; at most $\eta X$ for every $\eta > 0$ and large $q$ & \S\ref{sec:decomp} \\
$P^-(n)$; $\Phi(y, z)$ & the least prime factor; the number of $n \in (y, 2y]$ with no prime factor below $z$ &

  curve of the leaves' region & \S\ref{sec:inputs} \\
$\beta + i\gamma$ & a zero of $L(s, \chi)$ & \S\ref{sec:intro} \\

$S_Q(\chi, h)$; $f_0$ & the smoothed prime sum, of scale $Q$ and height $h$; its weight & \S\ref{sec:type2} \\
$W_i$, $P_i$, $V_i$; $W$, $P$, $V$ & the two Dirichlet polynomials, their lengths, their squared norms; the same

$m_i$; $s_i$; $\rho$ & $\log_q P_i$; $1 - m_i$, the shortfall of $P_i$ below $q$; $L - m_1 - m_2$, the rest's exponent &

$m$ & the cofactor, an integer & \S\ref{sec:type1} \\
$s_j$ & $\log_q Q_j$, the exponent of a prime sum's length & \S\ref{sec:window-estimate} \\
$\lambda$, $\mu$ & $27637/62500$, $1/12500$, the constants of the region & Def.~\ref{def:region} \\
$\rho_\pm$ & the integer ends of the rest's range & \S\ref{sec:tests} \\
$I$; $\beta_i$; $\mathcal X_i$ & $500000$; the bins' edges; the characters of the bin $i$ & \S\S\ref{sec:bins},

$\eta$ & any positive tolerance: in $o(X)$, in ``bounded at $C$'', in Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-two} &

$\delta$ & $10^{-6}$, in the bound for the prime sums on a bin; in an estimate with the bound $q^{L -

$\varpi$ & in the bin $i$, the excess over $\beta_i$ of the prime sums' exponent, $< 2.9 \cdot 10^{-6}$ &

  in the

$\delta'$ & a margin & Lem.~\ref{lem:leaves} \\
$M$; $E$ & an exponent of a mean square; the exponent of a bin's bound & \S\ref{sec:zone} \\
$E$ & the exponent of the rest's bound & Def.~\ref{def:fit} \\
$\ell_j(\chi)$; $t_j$; $\tau$ & the level of the $j$-th prime sum; its size; $10^{-6}$, the levels' step &

$\tau$ & in Proposition~\ref{prop:harman-fill}, the exponent of the rest's lower end & Prop.~\ref{prop:harman-fill} \\
$\gamma$ & the count exponent of a class & \S\ref{sec:window-estimate} \\
$\gamma$ & the room, $12 + \lfloor D/l_k \rfloor$ units, in the tests over a frozen part & \S\ref{sec:reversal} \\
$\mathfrak m_k$, $\mathfrak v_k$ & the counts' exponents, by the moments and by the large values &

$z_{\mathrm{top}}$; $z_f$; $f_*$ & $\lceil x^{(D/2 + 1)/D} \rceil$; the sifting level of the floor $f$; the root's floor

$\mathfrak b$; $\mathcal F(\mathfrak b)$; $l(\mathcal S)$, $h(\mathcal S)$ & a box, with cells $(l_i, h_i)$; its

  sums of $l_i$, of $h_i$ over $\mathcal S$ & \S\ref{sec:boxes} \\
$l_i$, $h_i$; $t$ & reals: $q^{l_i} \le r_i \le q^{h_i}$; $4q^t$ bounds the product of a leaf's new primes &
  Prop.~\ref{prop:harman-two} \\
$T(\mathcal C, \mathfrak b)$; $\Delta(\mathfrak b)$ & the term; its version in $\mathcal A$ less that in $\mathcal
  B/\varphi(q)$ & \S\ref{sec:boxes} \\
$\varepsilon$ & a sign, $\pm 1$ (elsewhere small and positive) & \S\ref{sec:boxes} \\
$\mathcal S_1$, $\mathcal S_2$ & the indices of the first and of the second variable & Lem.~\ref{lem:chains-two} \\
$\mathcal S_0$; $\mathcal S_1$ & the first variable's own primes; the primes of a leaf's block $d_1$ &

$r_1$, $r_2$ & the integers of (J3) & Def.~\ref{def:tests} \\
$r_1, \dots, r_j$; $r$ & the primes of a tuple; their product & \S\ref{sec:type1} \\
$d_1$, $d_2$; $a_1$, $a_2$ & a leaf's two blocks; their exponents of $x$ & Lem.~\ref{lem:leaves} \\
$w$ & $\lceil q^{\varepsilon_w} \rceil$, to which Brun's sieve sifts the leaves & Lem.~\ref{lem:leaves} \\

$\nu$; $\mathfrak c$ & $\lceil h_1/2 \rceil$, the level of the reversal; a chain & \S\ref{sec:reversal} \\

$c_*$ & the value of $\omega^*$ above $3$ & \S\ref{sec:dropped} \\
$A$, $B$; $A^*$, $B^*$ & the numerators of $a_1$, $a_2$; the cuts, for (L$_3$) & Def.~\ref{def:leaves3} \\

$c$ & a positive constant & Cor.~\ref{cor:count} \\
$f_*$; $c$ & at $43/20$, $49170839016$ and $1093510193343867034 < 0.94847\, \mathcal K$; at $21/10$, $33536647203$ and
  $1126600180626425749 < 0.97717\, \mathcal K$ & Prop.~\ref{prop:certificate}, \ref{prop:certificate-b} \\

  the others & \S\ref{sec:harman} \\
